English for Work · Health & life sciences

Medical Laboratory Technician English

Resolve specimen and order discrepancies, explain laboratory status precisely, report quality concerns, coordinate referral testing, and complete clear result-notification handoffs.

8 practical lessonsIntermediate to advanced1 complete learner book
Start practicing →

Words into action

Read the situation.
Find your words.
Make yourself clear.

For Medical laboratory technicians and clinical laboratory support staff.

Open learner book (PDF) ↗

Copy complete AI prompts ↓

What you will practice · Choose your pace

What you will practice

  • Resolve an accession-reference mismatch through the approved verification route without guessing identity or disclosing an unverified case.
  • Explain a documented specimen-quantity problem and relay a recollection request without inventing a collection method or clinical interpretation.
  • Explain the difference between completed analysis, unresolved quality review, and authorized reporting without supplying unsanctioned results.
  • Request a documented instrument-status decision and distinguish service attendance from authorized return to use.
  • Clarify a turnaround-time starting point and distinguish a quoted interval from a confirmed reporting promise during a backlog.

Choose your pace

Quick practice · 15 minutes: complete one short exchange, check the explanations, and read both roles aloud.

Extended practice · two or three sittings: check the case facts, study the vocabulary and phrases, complete the longer conversation, then rehearse the follow-up exchange.

Level guide: designed for intermediate to advanced learners. B1 learners can start with the short exchanges and supplied phrases; B2 learners can complete the cloze conversations; C1 learners can focus on the distinctions in the language checks. These are teaching suggestions, not a certified level assessment.

Your eight lessons

01 Accessioning and unambiguous identifiers02 Specimen suitability and recollection language03 Quality-control status and reportable results04 Reagents, instruments, and service coordination05 Turnaround times and realistic updates06 Preliminary, final, and amended reports07 Critical notifications and acknowledged handoffs08 Referral laboratories and outstanding work
Prompts

All cases and local procedures are fictional. This is English practice, not medical advice or laboratory operating instruction. Follow applicable privacy rules, validated procedures, professional scope, and authorized escalation. No exercise authorizes specimen collection, testing, instrument operation, result release, diagnosis, or treatment. The critical designation in Unit 7 belongs only to fictional Lumen; it is not a universal threshold. Patient identity and recipient authority must be verified through actual local arrangements.

01Accessioning and unambiguous identifiersA314 in the log, A341 in the request+

Your goal

Resolve an accession-reference mismatch through the approved verification route without guessing identity or disclosing an unverified case.

This lesson in the book, page 4 ↗

A Read the situation

A314 in the log, A341 in the request

The receiving log lists accession A314. Clinic caller Mira asks technician Noor about A341. No identity match between the request and the logged case has been established. Noor can check the accession log, but Mira must identify the requested case through the laboratory's privacy and verification arrangements. Similar digits do not establish a transcription error or authorize disclosure. The discussion ends with verification still required, not with a claimed case match.

Noor: Laboratory technician / Mira: Clinic caller

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. Which accession appears in the receiving log?

Answer and explanation

B. A314

The briefing identifies A314 as the accession in the receiving log.

2. Which reference does the caller request?

Answer and explanation

C. A341

Mira asks about A341, which differs from the logged A314.

3. What has been established about identity?

Answer and explanation

A. No match has been established

Similar digits do not establish a match, an error, or permission to disclose.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

A314 and A341 contain the same digits in a different order, but that resemblance does not prove they represent the same case. The log entry and caller request must remain distinct until the required verification establishes a match.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
accession
Laboratory registration of a specimen or request under its identifier.check the accession
accession number
Reference assigned to a registered laboratory case or specimen.read the accession number
accession log
Record of registered laboratory items.consult the accession log
receiving log
Record of items received by the laboratory.check the receiving log
requisition
Request document specifying laboratory testing information.review the requisition
patient identifier
Detail used under the approved process to identify a patient.verify patient identifiers
specimen identifier
Reference linking a specimen to its record.confirm the specimen identifier
case match
Established link between a request and the correct case.establish the case match
identifier mismatch
Difference between references that should be reconciled.report an identifier mismatch
transposed digits
Digits whose positions have been exchanged.avoid assuming transposed digits
transcription error
Mistake made while copying information.investigate a transcription error
verification
Check establishing required information under the applicable process.complete verification
caller identity
Identity of the person making an enquiry.verify caller identity
receiving authority
Permission for a person to receive particular information.verify receiving authority
confidentiality
Protection of information from unauthorized disclosure.maintain confidentiality
disclosure
Sharing information with a recipient.authorize disclosure
minimum necessary information
Information limited to what the applicable purpose and rules require.limit information sharing
secure channel
Approved communication route with relevant protection.use a secure channel
laboratory information system
System managing laboratory records; abbreviated LIS.consult the LIS
audit trail
Record showing actions and changes over time.preserve the audit trail
source record
Original or authoritative record for the fact being checked.consult the source record
unverified reference
Identifier not yet matched through the required checks.retain the unverified reference
clarification request
Question seeking missing or inconsistent information.raise a clarification request
identity resolution
Process of reconciling identifiers to the correct case.complete identity resolution

Do not disclose another accession as a suggested answer to an unverified caller. Repeat the reference the caller supplied, explain that verification is needed, and use the approved route for the necessary identifiers and authority checks.

C Notice the language

Slow down the identifier, not the standard

A caller may expect a quick answer because a reference looks nearly right. Be helpful by naming the verification step and repeating the supplied reference precisely. Do not offer another case as a likely match or let conversational confidence replace privacy checks.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Repeat the request
You are asking about A341.
Read it clearly
That is A, three, four, one.
State the limit
I have not established a verified case match.
Avoid guessing
I cannot assume a digit transposition.
Name the next step
We need to follow the laboratory's verification process.
Protect other records
I cannot discuss another case as a possible match.
Request the approved route
Please use the required verification channel.
Separate checks
Caller identity and receiving authority both need the applicable checks.
Consult the record
I can check the accession log.
Avoid automatic correction
I will not change the reference to make it fit.
Keep the enquiry traceable
I will retain the reference exactly as supplied.
Explain the purpose
This helps ensure we discuss the correct case.
Avoid a result claim
No case-specific status has been confirmed in this exchange.
Request clarification
Please confirm the requested case through the approved arrangements.
Preserve uncertainty
The reference remains unverified.
Close precisely
Verification is still required before case details can be discussed.
6 language and precision notes
As supplied
Preserves the caller's actual reference rather than a guessed correction.
Have not established
States the missing verification without disclosing another case.
Cannot assume
Rejects a shortcut while leaving investigation possible.
Both
Separates identity from authority to receive information.
Before
Makes disclosure dependent on the required verification.
Still required
Prevents the conversation from being mistaken for a completed match.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which response is appropriate before verification?

Answer and explanation

B. You asked about A341; please complete the required case verification

The appropriate response repeats the supplied reference and preserves the verification requirement.

2. What does the digit resemblance establish?

Answer and explanation

B. A possible mismatch requiring checking, not identity

Similar digits do not establish identity, responsibility for an error, or report status.

3. Which action preserves traceability?

Answer and explanation

A. Keep the supplied reference and document the unresolved enquiry

Retaining the actual request prevents a guessed correction from obscuring the enquiry.

4. Which final status is accurate?

Answer and explanation

B. Identity verification remains required

The scenario ends without an established case match or broader search conclusion.

Check explanations in the book, page 85 ↗

E Conversations

A314 in the log, A341 in the request

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Noor: Laboratory technician / Mira: Clinic caller

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • transcription error
  • unverified reference
  • secure channel
  • Confidentiality
  • accession log
  • audit trail
  • Receiving authority
  • verification
  • source record
  • case match
  1. Mira
    I have A341 on our request. Could you check the case status? I'm calling from the clinic.
  2. Noor
    You are asking about A, three, four, one. I need to complete the required 1 before discussing case details, and I have not established a match yet.
  3. Mira
    I may have transposed two digits. Could you tell me which other reference looks closest?
  4. Noor
    That is helpful. A possible 2 should be checked against the appropriate source rather than assumed. I will retain the number you actually supplied.
  5. Mira
    If you can search the log, why can't we just use the closest number and confirm it afterward?
  6. Noor
    I can consult the 3. That does not replace the case and recipient checks required by our process, and I cannot offer another case as a likely answer.
  7. Mira
    Understood. I'll keep our request separate until the required checks link it to the right case.
  8. Noor
    Exactly. The 4 remains unresolved. Similar digits are not enough to link records or authorize a case-specific status update.
  9. Mira
    I can use the usual protected verification route. Should I send the details through that process rather than this enquiry?
  10. Noor
    Yes, use the approved 5 and provide the information that process requires. We should not improvise a different identity check during this call.
  11. Mira
    I'll check the original requisition. A number I remember from another call may not be reliable.
  12. Noor
    The 6 is the right place to confirm what was requested. Please keep any correction traceable through the established arrangements.
  13. Mira
    Our clinic name is on the request. Does that also establish my authority to receive this particular information?
  14. Noor
    Correct. 7 needs the applicable check as well. We should keep the correct case and the permitted recipient clear before sharing its status.
  15. Mira
    I'll note that I asked about A341 and that verification is still required. I won't write case confirmed.
  16. Noor
    That preserves the 8. It also avoids turning a search or clarification conversation into evidence of a completed identity resolution.
  17. Mira
    If our source shows a correction, I'll send it through the established route. I won't silently replace the number in the enquiry.
  18. Noor
    Thank you. That supports the 9 without changing records simply to make two numbers agree.
  19. Mira
    I'll complete those checks next. For now, I have no case-specific result or status from you.
  20. Noor
    Agreed. 10 remains important throughout that process. I can assist with the enquiry once the required checks establish the correct case and authorized recipient.

Answers and explanations
  1. verification Turn 2. Verification must establish the correct case and permitted recipient before Noor discusses case-specific information.
  2. transcription error Turn 4. Transcription error is only a possibility, not an established explanation for the difference between references.
  3. accession log Turn 6. Accession log is the laboratory record Noor can check, without treating a similar entry as an authorized case match.
  4. case match Turn 8. Case match is the verified link between the clinic's enquiry and the correct laboratory record, which remains unestablished.
  5. secure channel Turn 10. Secure channel is the approved protected route for the required verification information rather than unrestricted disclosure in the enquiry.
  6. source record Turn 12. Source record provides the authoritative reference to check rather than relying on a remembered or guessed number.
  7. Receiving authority Turn 14. Receiving authority concerns permission to receive the specific information and is separate from merely claiming a clinic affiliation.
  8. unverified reference Turn 16. Unverified reference accurately describes A341 while the enquiry has not been linked to the correct case through the required checks.
  9. audit trail Turn 18. Audit trail preserves the supplied reference, subsequent correction, and verification actions instead of silently replacing the original request.
  10. Confidentiality Turn 20. Confidentiality requires protecting case information while the correct identity and recipient authority remain unresolved.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Mira: I have A341 on our request. Could you check the case status? I'm calling from the clinic.
  2. Noor: You are asking about A, three, four, one. I need to complete the required verification before discussing case details, and I have not established a match yet.
  3. Mira: I may have transposed two digits. Could you tell me which other reference looks closest?
  4. Noor: That is helpful. A possible transcription error should be checked against the appropriate source rather than assumed. I will retain the number you actually supplied.
  5. Mira: If you can search the log, why can't we just use the closest number and confirm it afterward?
  6. Noor: I can consult the accession log. That does not replace the case and recipient checks required by our process, and I cannot offer another case as a likely answer.
  7. Mira: Understood. I'll keep our request separate until the required checks link it to the right case.
  8. Noor: Exactly. The case match remains unresolved. Similar digits are not enough to link records or authorize a case-specific status update.
  9. Mira: I can use the usual protected verification route. Should I send the details through that process rather than this enquiry?
  10. Noor: Yes, use the approved secure channel and provide the information that process requires. We should not improvise a different identity check during this call.
  11. Mira: I'll check the original requisition. A number I remember from another call may not be reliable.
  12. Noor: The source record is the right place to confirm what was requested. Please keep any correction traceable through the established arrangements.
  13. Mira: Our clinic name is on the request. Does that also establish my authority to receive this particular information?
  14. Noor: Correct. Receiving authority needs the applicable check as well. We should keep the correct case and the permitted recipient clear before sharing its status.
  15. Mira: I'll note that I asked about A341 and that verification is still required. I won't write case confirmed.
  16. Noor: That preserves the unverified reference. It also avoids turning a search or clarification conversation into evidence of a completed identity resolution.
  17. Mira: If our source shows a correction, I'll send it through the established route. I won't silently replace the number in the enquiry.
  18. Noor: Thank you. That supports the audit trail without changing records simply to make two numbers agree.
  19. Mira: I'll complete those checks next. For now, I have no case-specific result or status from you.
  20. Noor: Agreed. Confidentiality remains important throughout that process. I can assist with the enquiry once the required checks establish the correct case and authorized recipient.
Dialogue in the book, page 10 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

A314 or A341

Fictional laboratory call using the accession case.

  1. Clinic caller: I'm asking about A341. Has it reached the laboratory, or is it still with the courier?
  2. Technician: Our log lists accession number A314. Let's clarify it before discussing specimen status.
  3. Clinic caller: Both came from our clinic, so I assumed the two references described the same case.
  4. Technician: The clinic location doesn't establish a match. Could you check the reference on your request?
  5. Clinic caller: The requisition says A314. I transposed the last two digits in my note.
  6. Technician: Thank you. Please repeat the full identifier so we can confirm the enquiry accurately.
  7. Clinic caller: A314. I'm asking whether that specimen's receipt is recorded, not whether a result is complete.
  8. Technician: Following our verification checks, I can confirm receipt for A314. Testing status is a separate question.
  9. Clinic caller: I'll correct my enquiry note to A314 and keep the question limited to receipt.
  10. Technician: That resolves the reference mismatch. Please use A314 for any further enquiry about this case.
An order without a registered specimen

Fictional electronic order exists, but laboratory receipt has not been confirmed.

  1. Clinic caller: The order appears in our system, so why can't I see an accession receipt?
  2. Technician: An electronic order and a received specimen are different things. I can see the request, not confirmed receipt.
  3. Clinic caller: Does that mean the specimen is lost somewhere between the clinic and the laboratory?
  4. Technician: We haven't established that. The order alone doesn't tell us whether collection or dispatch occurred.
  5. Clinic caller: I'll check the clinic's collection and courier records instead of assuming the laboratory has it.
  6. Technician: Please use the order reference when you check. That helps us compare the same enquiry.
  7. Clinic caller: Can you mark received while we look, just so the order stays visible to staff?
  8. Technician: Received would be inaccurate. The order can remain visible with specimen receipt unconfirmed.
  9. Clinic caller: Understood. I'll report that distinction to the clinician rather than say testing has begun.
  10. Technician: I'll keep the receipt enquiry open under the order reference while your clinic checks its records.
One request, an additional container

Fictional receiving enquiry about a second container; intended relationship is unresolved.

  1. Technician: A second container arrived with paperwork mentioning the same request. Was it intended as an additional specimen?
  2. Clinic coordinator: I need to check with the collection team. The courier sheet doesn't explain the relationship.
  3. Technician: Please don't infer that it replaces the first specimen simply because it arrived later.
  4. Clinic coordinator: I won't. Do you need the intended relationship or confirmation of a particular test result?
  5. Technician: The relationship is the question. No result status is being discussed in this call.
  6. Clinic coordinator: I'll ask the collection team which request the second container was intended to accompany.
  7. Technician: Our receiving lead will review the specimen label and paperwork before resolving this accessioning discrepancy.
  8. Clinic coordinator: Should I describe both as duplicates in the clinic note while we check?
  9. Technician: Not yet. Duplicate would be a conclusion about their relationship, which remains unresolved.
  10. Clinic coordinator: I'll describe a second container with an unclear relationship and get the collection team's account.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

A314 in the log, A341 in the request
  1. Check the completed dialogue, then read A341 as A, three, four, one. Do not offer the other logged accession as a possible patient match.
  2. Switch roles. Repeat the lines that require case verification and receiving authority before disclosure; retain the supplied request unchanged.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Read the reference without guessing

Complete the internal training summary. These record references are not authorization to disclose a real case.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Technician: "The receiving log lists ___."

Answer and explanation

D. A314

A314 is the reference explicitly supplied for the receiving log.

2. Caller: "The requested reference is ___."

Answer and explanation

D. A341

Mira supplied A341, which differs from the logged accession.

3. Technician: "The case match remains ___."

Answer and explanation

B. unverified

No required verification has established that the two references identify the same case.

4. Caller: "I will follow the approved verification ___."

Answer and explanation

D. process

The requested case must be identified through the laboratory's applicable arrangements.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

Lesson 01: Accessioning and unambiguous identifiers
Original fictional case: The receiving log lists accession A314. Clinic caller Mira asks technician Noor about A341. No identity match between the request and the logged case has been established. Noor can check the accession log, but Mira must identify the requested case through the laboratory's privacy and verification arrangements. Similar digits do not establish a transcription error or authorize disclosure. The discussion ends with verification still required, not with a claimed case match.
Language workshop: A314 in the log, A341 in the request
Communication goal: Resolve an accession-reference mismatch through the approved verification route without guessing identity or disclosing an unverified case.
Language guidance: A314 and A341 contain the same digits in a different order, but that resemblance does not prove they represent the same case. The log entry and caller request must remain distinct until the required verification establishes a match.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): You are asking about A341. / That is A, three, four, one. / I have not established a verified case match. / I cannot assume a digit transposition. / We need to follow the laboratory's verification process. / I cannot discuss another case as a possible match. / Please use the required verification channel. / Caller identity and receiving authority both need the applicable checks. / I can check the accession log. / I will not change the reference to make it fit. / I will retain the reference exactly as supplied. / This helps ensure we discuss the correct case. / No case-specific status has been confirmed in this exchange. / Please confirm the requested case through the approved arrangements. / The reference remains unverified. / Verification is still required before case details can be discussed.

Repeat the lines that require case verification and receiving authority before disclosure; retain the supplied request unchanged. Complete the four-line exchange. Practice grammar in context — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.
2. Give one brief occupational exchange containing a gap and four complete options A-D. Supply enough context for exactly one answer. Keep distractors plausible; distinguish grammar errors from wording that changes the facts. Stop and wait.
3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Communication goal: Resolve an accession-reference mismatch through the approved verification route without guessing identity or disclosing an unverified case.
Language guidance: A314 and A341 contain the same digits in a different order, but that resemblance does not prove they represent the same case. The log entry and caller request must remain distinct until the required verification establishes a match.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): You are asking about A341. / That is A, three, four, one. / I have not established a verified case match. / I cannot assume a digit transposition. / We need to follow the laboratory's verification process. / I cannot discuss another case as a possible match. / Please use the required verification channel. / Caller identity and receiving authority both need the applicable checks. / I can check the accession log. / I will not change the reference to make it fit. / I will retain the reference exactly as supplied. / This helps ensure we discuss the correct case. / No case-specific status has been confirmed in this exchange. / Please confirm the requested case through the approved arrangements. / The reference remains unverified. / Verification is still required before case details can be discussed.
Vocabulary: accession: Laboratory registration of a specimen or request under its identifier. / accession number: Reference assigned to a registered laboratory case or specimen. / accession log: Record of registered laboratory items. / receiving log: Record of items received by the laboratory. / requisition: Request document specifying laboratory testing information. / patient identifier: Detail used under the approved process to identify a patient. / specimen identifier: Reference linking a specimen to its record. / case match: Established link between a request and the correct case. / identifier mismatch: Difference between references that should be reconciled. / transposed digits: Digits whose positions have been exchanged. / transcription error: Mistake made while copying information. / verification: Check establishing required information under the applicable process. / caller identity: Identity of the person making an enquiry. / receiving authority: Permission for a person to receive particular information. / confidentiality: Protection of information from unauthorized disclosure. / disclosure: Sharing information with a recipient. / minimum necessary information: Information limited to what the applicable purpose and rules require. / secure channel: Approved communication route with relevant protection. / laboratory information system: System managing laboratory records; abbreviated LIS. / audit trail: Record showing actions and changes over time. / source record: Original or authoritative record for the fact being checked. / unverified reference: Identifier not yet matched through the required checks. / clarification request: Question seeking missing or inconsistent information. / identity resolution: Process of reconciling identifiers to the correct case.
Speaking task: Check the completed dialogue, then read A341 as A, three, four, one. Do not offer the other logged accession as a possible patient match. Switch roles. Repeat the lines that require case verification and receiving authority before disclosure; retain the supplied request unchanged. Complete the four-line exchange. Confirm that A314 belongs to the receiving log and A341 to the caller's request, with the match still unverified.
Partner: Noor: Laboratory technician / Mira: Clinic caller
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

02Specimen suitability and recollection languageInsufficient quantity is not a low patient result+

Your goal

Explain a documented specimen-quantity problem and relay a recollection request without inventing a collection method or clinical interpretation.

This lesson in the book, page 13 ↗

A Read the situation

Insufficient quantity is not a low patient result

The laboratory lead has documented insufficient quantity for test request Q6 and requested recollection through the clinic. No cause, collection method, or urgency is supplied. Clinic coordinator Alex thinks insufficient quantity means the patient has a low test result. Technician Noor corrects that misunderstanding and relays only the documented operational message. Further collection or urgency questions need the appropriate authorized guidance, not a guessed instruction.

Noor: Laboratory technician / Alex: Clinic coordinator

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What does the lead document for Q6?

Answer and explanation

A. Insufficient specimen quantity

The documented finding concerns the amount available for the requested test.

2. What action has the lead requested?

Answer and explanation

B. Recollection through the clinic

The scenario explicitly supplies a recollection request through the clinic.

3. Which detail is not supplied?

Answer and explanation

C. Collection method

No collection method, urgency, or cause is included in the stated facts.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Insufficient quantity refers to specimen amount for Q6. It is not a numerical test result and does not mean the patient's measured concentration is low. Do not substitute a clinical conclusion for an operational suitability message.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
specimen
Material submitted for laboratory examination.identify the specimen
specimen suitability
Whether the submitted material meets the relevant test requirements.assess specimen suitability
insufficient quantity
Available amount below what the requested test requires here.document insufficient quantity
quantity not sufficient
Common laboratory expression for insufficient material; abbreviated QNS.clarify a QNS message
recollection
Obtaining another specimen under the applicable instructions.request recollection
test request
Order identifying the examination being requested.quote the test request
specimen volume
Amount of submitted material.distinguish specimen volume
analyte
Substance or property being measured by a test.name the analyte
measured concentration
Reported amount of an analyte relative to a unit quantity.distinguish measured concentration
patient result
Reported finding from the patient's test.avoid inventing a patient result
collection method
Approved way the required specimen is obtained.confirm the collection method
collection container
Receptacle specified for a specimen under the relevant procedure.verify the collection container
specimen requirement
Condition the submitted material must meet for a test.consult specimen requirements
rejection reason
Documented reason a specimen cannot be accepted for the requested work.relay the rejection reason
preanalytical phase
Work before the analytical measurement, including specimen handling.discuss a preanalytical issue
aliquot
Portion separated from a specimen for a defined purpose.identify an aliquot
hemolysis
Breakdown of red blood cells that can affect specimen assessment.report documented hemolysis
clotted specimen
Specimen containing clotting where relevant to suitability.describe a documented clotted specimen
contamination
Unwanted material introduced into a specimen.investigate documented contamination
stability
Ability of a specimen or analyte to remain suitable under defined conditions.check stability requirements
transport condition
Required condition during specimen movement.confirm transport conditions
recollection request
Documented request for another specimen.relay the recollection request
clinical interpretation
Explanation of a result's meaning for patient care.refer clinical interpretation
authorized guidance
Instructions from the role or procedure permitted to provide them.obtain authorized guidance

The lead requested recollection through the clinic. That request supplies no method, cause, or urgency. Relay the stated action and seek the appropriate authorized guidance for missing details rather than inventing instructions.

C Notice the language

Correct the meaning before adding detail

A familiar word such as low can move a conversation from specimen status into an unsupported patient conclusion. State what the term refers to, repeat the documented action, and keep missing clinical or collection details separate.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Identify the request
This message concerns test request Q6.
Attribute the finding
The laboratory lead documented insufficient quantity.
Explain the term
It refers to the amount of specimen available for testing.
Correct the misunderstanding
It does not mean the patient has a low result.
Avoid inventing a value
No patient result is supplied in this message.
Relay the action
The lead requested recollection through the clinic.
Keep cause separate
The cause is not documented here.
Avoid blame
I cannot attribute this to a collection error.
Name missing instructions
No collection method is included in this message.
Seek the right guidance
Please obtain the applicable authorized collection instructions.
Keep urgency separate
No urgency classification is supplied here.
Avoid a timing promise
I cannot confirm a new report time from this message.
Preserve the wording
Please retain insufficient quantity in the record.
Check understanding
Are we clear that this is a specimen-amount issue?
Limit the handoff
I am relaying the documented request, not giving a diagnosis.
Close accurately
Q6 needs recollection through the clinic under the appropriate guidance.
6 language and precision notes
Refers to
Defines the subject of the quantity statement.
Does not mean
Corrects the specific clinical misunderstanding.
Documented
Attributes the finding to the supplied record.
Not included
Marks missing instructions without guessing them.
Through the clinic
Preserves the stated route for the recollection request.
Not giving
Separates operational communication from clinical interpretation.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which explanation is accurate?

Answer and explanation

B. There is insufficient specimen quantity for Q6, not a low patient result

The message concerns suitability of the available specimen amount only.

2. Which action is documented?

Answer and explanation

C. Recollection through the clinic

Only recollection through the clinic is stated as the lead's requested action.

3. Which statement wrongly adds a cause?

Answer and explanation

C. The collector definitely used the wrong container

The scenario supplies no cause, so blaming a container or collector is unsupported.

4. How should urgency be described?

Answer and explanation

D. Not supplied in this message; follow the applicable escalation arrangements

No urgency classification is given, so neither routine nor critical may be invented.

E Conversations

Insufficient quantity is not a low patient result

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Noor: Laboratory technician / Alex: Clinic coordinator

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • clinical interpretation
  • patient result
  • authorized guidance
  • recollection
  • specimen volume
  • recollection request
  • rejection reason
  • test request
  • preanalytical phase
  • specimen requirement
  1. Alex
    I'm updating the clinic's Q6 note. It says insufficient quantity. Should I tell the clinician the patient's result is low?
  2. Noor
    No. It refers to 1, not the patient's concentration. The lead documented too little material for the requested test and asked for recollection through the clinic.
  3. Alex
    I'll remove low result. Which reference should stay with the message so the team links it to the right order?
  4. Noor
    Keep 2 Q6 in the note. The quantity message and the requested follow-up both belong to that request.
  5. Alex
    The result field is blank. Should I enter zero until the replacement specimen arrives?
  6. Noor
    No. No 3 is supplied. A blank result field does not mean the measured value is zero.
  7. Alex
    Understood. The action is to obtain another specimen. Can you confirm that is actually what the lead requested?
  8. Noor
    Yes, the documented request is 4 through the clinic. That is the action supplied, not an instruction to use a particular collection method.
  9. Alex
    Someone has suggested the collector used the wrong container. Is that documented anywhere in this message?
  10. Noor
    No. The stated 5 is insufficient quantity. It does not establish a container error or identify who caused the shortage.
  11. Alex
    So I can report a suitability issue without recording collector error. Is this a problem before the analytical measurement rather than a low concentration?
  12. Noor
    Yes. This is a 6 issue about the submitted material. It is not a patient finding from an analytical result.
  13. Alex
    The clinic will ask whether recollection is routine or urgent. Neither label appears in the message I have.
  14. Noor
    Obtain the applicable 7 through the actual escalation arrangements. Don't assign a clinical urgency from the quantity wording alone.
  15. Alex
    I also need the collection instructions. I should not choose a container or amount simply to complete the note.
  16. Noor
    Correct. Check the relevant 8 through the authorized procedure. This message does not provide those instructions or a new report time.
  17. Alex
    I'll relay Q6, insufficient specimen quantity, and the lead's request to recollect through the clinic. Missing instructions go to the appropriate contact.
  18. Noor
    That preserves the 9. It also removes the unsupported low-result statement without replacing it with a guessed cause.
  19. Alex
    The clinician may still ask what this means for the patient. I'll distinguish that question from the specimen-status message.
  20. Noor
    Yes. Refer 10 to the appropriately qualified professional with the actual clinical context. Our note should preserve the documented limitation and requested follow-up.

Answers and explanations
  1. specimen volume Turn 2. Specimen volume is the amount available for testing, not the patient's measured test result.
  2. test request Turn 4. Test request Q6 identifies the work associated with the documented quantity issue and recollection request.
  3. patient result Turn 6. Patient result has not been supplied, so Noor must not invent a value or a low-result interpretation.
  4. recollection Turn 8. Recollection is the lead's requested next action through the clinic, without a supplied method or urgency classification.
  5. rejection reason Turn 10. Rejection reason here is insufficient quantity for Q6, which does not establish who caused it or how it happened.
  6. preanalytical phase Turn 12. Preanalytical phase concerns work before measurement, and this specimen issue must not be recast as an analytical patient finding.
  7. authorized guidance Turn 14. Authorized guidance is needed for missing urgency or collection details rather than inventing a classification from the quantity message.
  8. specimen requirement Turn 16. Specimen requirement must be established from the relevant test instructions, not inferred into a collection or reporting promise.
  9. recollection request Turn 18. Recollection request is the documented action to obtain another specimen through the clinic without additional invented details.
  10. clinical interpretation Turn 20. Clinical interpretation concerns patient meaning and is not provided by this specimen-quantity communication.
Read the complete conversation

  11. Alex: So I can report a suitability issue without recording collector error. Is this a problem before the analytical measurement rather than a low concentration?

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Too little specimen, not a low result

Fictional Q6 call using the lesson case.

  1. Clinic coordinator: The note says insufficient quantity. Does that mean the patient's measured level was low?
  2. Technician: No. It describes the amount of specimen available for Q6, not a measured patient result.
  3. Clinic coordinator: So there isn't a number I should pass to the clinician from this request?
  4. Technician: Correct. Our acceptance criteria aren't met. The lead requested recollection for Q6; no result is reportable.
  5. Clinic coordinator: Does the request tell us when or how to collect another specimen?
  6. Technician: This call provides no collection instructions. Your care team needs to arrange that under its procedures.
  7. Clinic coordinator: I'll pass the documented request to the clinician rather than tell the patient a result was low.
  8. Technician: Please include Q6 so the team knows which request the recollection message concerns.
  9. Clinic coordinator: Can we say the original collector caused the insufficient amount to avoid confusion?
  10. Technician: The cause isn't established. Report the documented quantity issue without assigning blame for it.
Hemolysis is not a diagnosis

Fictional enquiry about a specimen comment; method-specific assessment remains with the laboratory lead.

  1. Clinic nurse: The specimen comment mentions hemolysis. Can you tell me what the patient has?
  2. Technician: The comment describes a specimen condition. It isn't, by itself, a diagnosis of the patient.
  3. Clinic nurse: Does it mean every test from that collection must be considered unusable?
  4. Technician: That depends on the laboratory's assessment for the relevant tests. I can't generalize from this comment.
  5. Clinic nurse: Could you ask the laboratory lead which requested tests the recorded decision covers?
  6. Technician: Yes. I'll ask for the scope of the decision under the accession reference.
  7. Clinic nurse: Please don't send a general recollection message if only one request is affected.
  8. Technician: I'll relay the lead's documented decision, including its scope, rather than invent broader instructions.
  9. Clinic nurse: We need that distinction before we explain the situation to the ordering clinician.
  10. Technician: I'll mark your question as decision scope and ask the lead to clarify it with your team.
Recollection requested, arrival unknown

Fictional specimen-status call; clinic says a new collection was planned, not confirmed.

  1. Technician: We're following up on the recollection request for S12. Has a new specimen been obtained?
  2. Clinic coordinator: It was planned for this morning, but I haven't confirmed whether the appointment took place.
  3. Technician: Then collection and laboratory receipt both remain unconfirmed. I'll avoid marking a new specimen received.
  4. Clinic coordinator: Could you tell the clinician that the plan is in place, even though completion is unknown?
  5. Technician: Yes, provided I label it planned. That wording doesn't imply the collection actually happened.
  6. Clinic coordinator: I'll check the attendance and collection records with our team and return the verified status.
  7. Technician: Please use S12 and distinguish the original specimen from any replacement in your reply.
  8. Clinic coordinator: Should I say the original request is resolved because we've arranged the appointment?
  9. Technician: No. The new appointment doesn't establish a suitable replacement or completed testing.
  10. Clinic coordinator: Understood. I'll report the confirmed stage after checking, rather than close the recollection enquiry early.

  2. Switch roles and read the recollection message for Q6. Keep collection method, cause and urgency unspecified, as supplied.

Language guidance: Insufficient quantity refers to specimen amount for Q6. It is not a numerical test result and does not mean the patient's measured concentration is low. Do not substitute a clinical conclusion for an operational suitability message.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): This message concerns test request Q6. / The laboratory lead documented insufficient quantity. / It refers to the amount of specimen available for testing. / It does not mean the patient has a low result. / No patient result is supplied in this message. / The lead requested recollection through the clinic. / The cause is not documented here. / I cannot attribute this to a collection error. / No collection method is included in this message. / Please obtain the applicable authorized collection instructions. / No urgency classification is supplied here. / I cannot confirm a new report time from this message. / Please retain insufficient quantity in the record. / Are we clear that this is a specimen-amount issue? / I am relaying the documented request, not giving a diagnosis. / Q6 needs recollection through the clinic under the appropriate guidance.
Vocabulary: specimen: Material submitted for laboratory examination. / specimen suitability: Whether the submitted material meets the relevant test requirements. / insufficient quantity: Available amount below what the requested test requires here. / quantity not sufficient: Common laboratory expression for insufficient material; abbreviated QNS. / recollection: Obtaining another specimen under the applicable instructions. / test request: Order identifying the examination being requested. / specimen volume: Amount of submitted material. / analyte: Substance or property being measured by a test. / measured concentration: Reported amount of an analyte relative to a unit quantity. / patient result: Reported finding from the patient's test. / collection method: Approved way the required specimen is obtained. / collection container: Receptacle specified for a specimen under the relevant procedure. / specimen requirement: Condition the submitted material must meet for a test. / rejection reason: Documented reason a specimen cannot be accepted for the requested work. / preanalytical phase: Work before the analytical measurement, including specimen handling. / aliquot: Portion separated from a specimen for a defined purpose. / hemolysis: Breakdown of red blood cells that can affect specimen assessment. / clotted specimen: Specimen containing clotting where relevant to suitability. / contamination: Unwanted material introduced into a specimen. / stability: Ability of a specimen or analyte to remain suitable under defined conditions. / transport condition: Required condition during specimen movement. / recollection request: Documented request for another specimen. / clinical interpretation: Explanation of a result's meaning for patient care. / authorized guidance: Instructions from the role or procedure permitted to provide them.

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.
2. Give one brief occupational exchange containing a gap and four complete options A-D. Supply enough context for exactly one answer. Keep distractors plausible; distinguish grammar errors from wording that changes the facts. Stop and wait.
3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

03Quality-control status and reportable resultsThe run is complete; release is not authorized+

Your goal

Explain the difference between completed analysis, unresolved quality review, and authorized reporting without supplying unsanctioned results.

This lesson in the book, page 22 ↗

A Read the situation

The run is complete; release is not authorized

An analytical run has finished, but its quality-control review remains unresolved. The laboratory lead has not authorized result release. Clinic contact Dana asks technician Noor to describe the results as final because the instrument has completed its work. No patient values, quality findings, or troubleshooting instructions are supplied. Noor must report the actual status and refer the unresolved review to the laboratory lead without relabeling or releasing results.

Noor: Laboratory technician / Dana: Clinic contact

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. Which stage is complete?

Answer and explanation

C. The analytical run

The instrument has finished the analytical run, but review and release remain unresolved.

2. What is the quality-control status?

Answer and explanation

A. Review unresolved

No resolved quality-control finding is supplied, so neither pass nor failure may be assumed.

3. Which release claim is supported?

Answer and explanation

B. The lead has not authorized release

The run is complete, but only the stated laboratory process can authorize release; no such permission is supplied.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Run complete is an instrument-workflow status. It does not establish resolved quality control or release authorization. Because review is unresolved, do not describe the quality outcome as passed, failed, or irrelevant.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
analytical run
Defined set of measurements performed in one testing sequence.complete the analytical run
quality control
Checks used to monitor analytical performance; abbreviated QC.review quality control
control material
Material with defined characteristics used to assess analytical performance.identify control material
control result
Measurement obtained from a quality-control material.review a control result
acceptance limit
Defined boundary used to assess an applicable control condition.apply the approved acceptance limit
quality review
Authorized assessment of relevant quality evidence.await quality review
release authorization
Permission to issue results under the applicable process.obtain release authorization
reportable result
Result meeting the applicable requirements for reporting.distinguish a reportable result
standard deviation
Measure of spread around the mean; abbreviated SD.express a difference in SD units
pending review
Status indicating an assessment is not yet resolved.retain pending review
z-score
Difference from a specified mean divided by the specified SD.calculate the control z-score
report status
Current stage of a report in its lifecycle.state report status
precision
Closeness among repeated measurements under specified conditions.discuss analytical precision
accuracy
Closeness of a measurement to an accepted reference value.distinguish accuracy from precision
bias
Systematic difference from an appropriate reference.investigate documented bias
random variation
Unpredictable spread in measurements under relevant conditions.assess random variation
trend
Directional pattern across observations over time.identify a control trend
shift
Sustained change in the level of observed measurements.investigate a control shift
out-of-control condition
Quality state outside the applicable accepted criteria.report a documented out-of-control condition
coefficient of variation
SD divided by the mean, usually expressed as a percentage; CV.report the CV with its basis
withheld result
Result not released pending the applicable process.preserve withheld-result status
review outcome
Documented conclusion of an assessment.request the review outcome
authorized reviewer
Person permitted to assess and approve the relevant evidence.consult the authorized reviewer
status update
Message reporting the current process stage.give a status update

The clinic may need an update, but its request does not supply the missing authorization. Give the current stage and route the review question to the laboratory lead. No values or technical corrective actions are part of this exchange.

C Notice the language

A completed stage is not a completed process

Under time pressure, a caller may use finished to mean ready to report. Repeat the completed stage, then name the outstanding review and authority. This acknowledges progress without allowing the caller to infer a final report that does not exist.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Acknowledge completion
The analytical run has finished.
Name the unresolved stage
Quality-control review remains unresolved.
State the authority limit
The lead has not authorized result release.
Correct the inference
Instrument completion does not make the report final.
Avoid an invented QC outcome
I cannot describe the review as passed or failed.
Offer the actual status
I can provide a process-status update.
Keep values protected
I am not releasing patient values in this exchange.
Refer the review
The laboratory lead needs to resolve the review.
Avoid a deadline promise
No release time is confirmed.
Separate stages
Analysis, review, and release are different steps.
Preserve the wording
Please retain pending review in the update.
Resist relabeling
I cannot relabel unreleased results as final.
Attribute the next decision
Release depends on the authorized process.
Avoid technical improvisation
I am not supplying troubleshooting instructions.
Check the handoff
Please confirm that final reporting has not been authorized.
Close accurately
Run complete, review unresolved, release not authorized.
6 language and precision notes
Remains
Shows that completion of one stage did not resolve another.
Does not make
Blocks the shortcut from instrument status to final report.
Passed or failed
Names two unsupported conclusions, not two available choices.
Depends on
Preserves the required authorization relationship.
Not confirmed
Avoids turning a requested deadline into a promise.
Relabel
Identifies a wording change that would misrepresent actual status.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which update is accurate?

Answer and explanation

A. The run is complete, QC review is unresolved, and release is not authorized

The correct update preserves all three distinct process and authorization states.

2. What does unresolved QC review establish?

Answer and explanation

A. No completed review outcome is supplied

An unresolved review supports neither a pass nor a failure conclusion.

3. Who should address the review question?

Answer and explanation

B. The laboratory lead under the authorized process

The stated release and review authority belongs to the laboratory process and lead.

4. Which action misrepresents status?

Answer and explanation

A. Calling the results final without authorization

Final status cannot be created merely by changing the wording of an unreleased result.

Check explanations in the book, page 89 ↗

E Conversations

The run is complete; release is not authorized

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Noor: Laboratory technician / Dana: Clinic contact

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • quality control
  • status update
  • report status
  • analytical run
  • review outcome
  • release authorization
  • authorized reviewer
  • reportable result
  • pending review
  • withheld result
  1. Dana
    The instrument has finished. Can I tell the clinic these are final results? They're waiting for an answer.
  2. Noor
    The 1 is complete, but quality review remains unresolved. The laboratory lead has not authorized release.
  3. Dana
    I'll remove final from the update. What wording should I use for the stage that is still outstanding?
  4. Noor
    Use 2. That describes the unresolved assessment without suggesting the controls passed or failed.
  5. Dana
    I was about to call it a QC failure because nothing is being released. Is that too strong?
  6. Noor
    Yes. No 3 is supplied. An unresolved assessment is not the same as a documented failure.
  7. Dana
    The clinic still needs a message. Can we give them the process status without including patient values?
  8. Noor
    Yes. A 4 can say analysis completed, review unresolved, release not authorized. It must not imply a final report exists.
  9. Dana
    Would the clinic's request to treat it as final count as permission? They say they accept that review is pending.
  10. Noor
    No. 5 must come through the laboratory's applicable process. The request does not replace the lead's decision.
  11. Dana
    Then the next question is who can resolve the review, rather than whether I can change the label.
  12. Noor
    Correct. The 6 needs to assess the relevant evidence. I can pass the enquiry to the laboratory lead without inventing a technical conclusion.
  13. Dana
    If I leave final off but say available, could that still suggest the results are ready to use?
  14. Noor
    It could. Keep the 7 explicit: not released, not final. A softer label should not hide the outstanding requirement.
  15. Dana
    Understood. The completed measurement stage alone does not establish that the result meets the reporting requirements.
  16. Noor
    Exactly. A 8 requires those requirements to be met; instrument completion alone is not enough.
  17. Dana
    Is there any supplied indication that the review can be skipped because the clinic is under time pressure?
  18. Noor
    No. The unresolved 9 remains part of the process. We have no authorization to bypass it or provide troubleshooting instructions.
  19. Dana
    I'll send the process update, remove the final claim, and direct the release-status enquiry to the lead.
  20. Noor
    Good. Keep any 10 unreleased until the applicable review and authorization are resolved. A clear update can acknowledge the delay without supplying unsanctioned findings.

Answers and explanations
  1. analytical run Turn 2. Analytical run completion confirms that the measurement sequence finished, not that review or release has been authorized.
  2. pending review Turn 4. Pending review accurately states the unresolved quality assessment without suggesting it has passed or failed.
  3. review outcome Turn 6. Review outcome is not supplied, so an unresolved assessment cannot be reported as a confirmed pass or failure.
  4. status update Turn 8. Status update communicates the current workflow stage without inventing patient findings or an analytical conclusion.
  5. release authorization Turn 10. Release authorization must come through the applicable laboratory process, not from the clinic's request for final wording.
  6. authorized reviewer Turn 12. Authorized reviewer is the role that must resolve the quality assessment under the laboratory process before a release claim is made.
  7. report status Turn 14. Report status remains unreleased and not final, even though the instrument's analytical stage is complete.
  8. reportable result Turn 16. Reportable result must meet the relevant reporting requirements, which are not established by instrument completion alone.
  9. quality control Turn 18. Quality control is the analytical-performance review area still unresolved, not an optional step erased by the completed run.
  10. withheld result Turn 20. Withheld result describes information not released pending the applicable review and authorization rather than a final report.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Dana: The instrument has finished. Can I tell the clinic these are final results? They're waiting for an answer.
  2. Noor: The analytical run is complete, but quality review remains unresolved. The laboratory lead has not authorized release.
  3. Dana: I'll remove final from the update. What wording should I use for the stage that is still outstanding?
  4. Noor: Use pending review. That describes the unresolved assessment without suggesting the controls passed or failed.
  5. Dana: I was about to call it a QC failure because nothing is being released. Is that too strong?
  6. Noor: Yes. No review outcome is supplied. An unresolved assessment is not the same as a documented failure.
  7. Dana: The clinic still needs a message. Can we give them the process status without including patient values?
  8. Noor: Yes. A status update can say analysis completed, review unresolved, release not authorized. It must not imply a final report exists.
  9. Dana: Would the clinic's request to treat it as final count as permission? They say they accept that review is pending.
  10. Noor: No. release authorization must come through the laboratory's applicable process. The request does not replace the lead's decision.
  11. Dana: Then the next question is who can resolve the review, rather than whether I can change the label.
  12. Noor: Correct. The authorized reviewer needs to assess the relevant evidence. I can pass the enquiry to the laboratory lead without inventing a technical conclusion.
  13. Dana: If I leave final off but say available, could that still suggest the results are ready to use?
  14. Noor: It could. Keep the report status explicit: not released, not final. A softer label should not hide the outstanding requirement.
  15. Dana: Understood. The completed measurement stage alone does not establish that the result meets the reporting requirements.
  16. Noor: Exactly. A reportable result requires those requirements to be met; instrument completion alone is not enough.
  17. Dana: Is there any supplied indication that the review can be skipped because the clinic is under time pressure?
  18. Noor: No. The unresolved quality control remains part of the process. We have no authorization to bypass it or provide troubleshooting instructions.
  19. Dana: I'll send the process update, remove the final claim, and direct the release-status enquiry to the lead.
  20. Noor: Good. Keep any withheld result unreleased until the applicable review and authorization are resolved. A clear update can acknowledge the delay without supplying unsanctioned findings.

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Finished on the instrument, awaiting review

Fictional clinic enquiry using the quality-control case.

  1. Clinic caller: The analyzer has finished, so can you send the final results to our team?
  2. Technician: The analytical run is complete, but quality-control review is unresolved and release hasn't been authorized.
  3. Clinic caller: Our clinician is waiting. Could you at least call the results final on the status screen?
  4. Technician: Final would be inaccurate. I can report completed testing with review and release still outstanding.
  5. Clinic caller: Does that mean all the numbers must be wrong, or that nobody has looked?
  6. Technician: Neither conclusion follows from this status. The laboratory lead is reviewing the issue.
  7. Clinic caller: When will that review finish? We need a definite time for the patient.
  8. Technician: I don't have a confirmed release time. I'll pass the timing concern to the lead.
  9. Clinic caller: Please make the difference explicit in your update, so reception doesn't promise a finished report.
  10. Technician: I will: run complete, quality review unresolved, and no release authorization confirmed.
Control material is not a patient result

Fictional new colleague misreads the source of an entry; no numerical results.

  1. Technician: The entry you quoted is labeled control material, not a patient specimen result.
  2. Colleague: I saw it beside the run record and assumed it belonged to the patient accession.
  3. Technician: The control entry monitors the testing process. Its position doesn't make it a patient result.
  4. Colleague: Then I shouldn't copy that entry into a patient-status message or discuss its clinical meaning.
  5. Technician: Correct. First establish the source and record type; keep the run review with the lead.
  6. Colleague: Could you show me the label that distinguishes the two records, without interpreting the control?
  7. Technician: The source field says control material here; the patient record uses its accession reference.
  8. Colleague: I see the distinction now. My question concerns record identity, not whether performance is acceptable.
  9. Technician: I'll ask the lead to review any performance question. We've resolved which entry you were reading.
  10. Colleague: Thanks. I'll withdraw the unsent patient-status wording and check the correct accession record instead.
A run includes more than one request

Fictional result-status query; one reviewed request does not establish the whole run's status.

  1. Clinic nurse: We received a final report for one request. Does that mean everything in the run is released?
  2. Technician: Not necessarily. A final report identifies its own request; it doesn't confirm every other request's status.
  3. Clinic nurse: Could you check the two accession references separately rather than answer about the instrument generally?
  4. Technician: Yes. The first shows released; the second remains under review, with no release time confirmed.
  5. Clinic nurse: I had assumed the same instrument meant the same reporting stage for both.
  6. Technician: The records show otherwise here. Shared testing equipment doesn't make their statuses interchangeable.
  7. Clinic nurse: Please put the second reference first in the update, because that's the one our clinician needs clarified.
  8. Technician: I'll report its review status explicitly and keep the already released request separate.
  9. Clinic nurse: Should I say there's been a new failure, given that one report is delayed?
  10. Technician: No cause is supplied. A different release stage doesn't by itself establish a new failure.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

The run is complete; release is not authorized
  1. After checking the dialogue, read the three statuses aloud: analysis complete, quality review unresolved, release not authorized.
  2. Switch roles. Correct final to not released in the printed exchange; keep unresolved distinct from both QC passed and QC failed.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Three stages, three separate facts

Complete the clinic update using the actual run, review, and release status.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Technician: "The analytical run is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. complete

The instrument has finished the stated analytical sequence.

2. Clinic: "Quality-control review remains ___."

Answer and explanation

C. unresolved

No completed quality-control review outcome is supplied in this scenario.

3. Technician: "Result release is not ___."

Answer and explanation

C. authorized

The laboratory lead has not given permission to release results.

4. Clinic: "We must not call the report ___."

Answer and explanation

C. final

Final reporting is not established by the instrument's completed run.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.
