Lesson 03: Quality-control status and reportable results
Original fictional case: An analytical run has finished, but its quality-control review remains unresolved. The laboratory lead has not authorized result release. Clinic contact Dana asks technician Noor to describe the results as final because the instrument has completed its work. No patient values, quality findings, or troubleshooting instructions are supplied. Noor must report the actual status and refer the unresolved review to the laboratory lead without relabeling or releasing results.
Language workshop: The run is complete; release is not authorized
Communication goal: Explain the difference between completed analysis, unresolved quality review, and authorized reporting without supplying unsanctioned results.
Language guidance: Run complete is an instrument-workflow status. It does not establish resolved quality control or release authorization. Because review is unresolved, do not describe the quality outcome as passed, failed, or irrelevant.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): The analytical run has finished. / Quality-control review remains unresolved. / The lead has not authorized result release. / Instrument completion does not make the report final. / I cannot describe the review as passed or failed. / I can provide a process-status update. / I am not releasing patient values in this exchange. / The laboratory lead needs to resolve the review. / No release time is confirmed. / Analysis, review, and release are different steps. / Please retain pending review in the update. / I cannot relabel unreleased results as final. / Release depends on the authorized process. / I am not supplying troubleshooting instructions. / Please confirm that final reporting has not been authorized. / Run complete, review unresolved, release not authorized.
Vocabulary: analytical run: Defined set of measurements performed in one testing sequence. / quality control: Checks used to monitor analytical performance; abbreviated QC. / control material: Material with defined characteristics used to assess analytical performance. / control result: Measurement obtained from a quality-control material. / acceptance limit: Defined boundary used to assess an applicable control condition. / quality review: Authorized assessment of relevant quality evidence. / release authorization: Permission to issue results under the applicable process. / reportable result: Result meeting the applicable requirements for reporting. / standard deviation: Measure of spread around the mean; abbreviated SD. / pending review: Status indicating an assessment is not yet resolved. / z-score: Difference from a specified mean divided by the specified SD. / report status: Current stage of a report in its lifecycle. / precision: Closeness among repeated measurements under specified conditions. / accuracy: Closeness of a measurement to an accepted reference value. / bias: Systematic difference from an appropriate reference. / random variation: Unpredictable spread in measurements under relevant conditions. / trend: Directional pattern across observations over time. / shift: Sustained change in the level of observed measurements. / out-of-control condition: Quality state outside the applicable accepted criteria. / coefficient of variation: SD divided by the mean, usually expressed as a percentage; CV. / withheld result: Result not released pending the applicable process. / review outcome: Documented conclusion of an assessment. / authorized reviewer: Person permitted to assess and approve the relevant evidence. / status update: Message reporting the current process stage.
Speaking task: After checking the dialogue, read the three statuses aloud: analysis complete, quality review unresolved, release not authorized. Switch roles. Correct final to not released in the printed exchange; keep unresolved distinct from both QC passed and QC failed. Complete the four-line exchange. Retain the lead's authorization requirement and do not substitute the clinic's request for permission.
Partner: Noor: Laboratory technician / Dana: Clinic contact
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Complete the clinic update using the actual run, review, and release status.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Dana: Is there any supplied indication that the review can be skipped because the clinic is under time pressure?
Noor: No. The unresolved quality control remains part of the process. We have no authorization to bypass it or provide troubleshooting instructions.
Dana: I'll send the process update, remove the final claim, and direct the release-status enquiry to the lead.
Noor: Good. Keep any withheld result unreleased until the applicable review and authorization are resolved. A clear update can acknowledge the delay without supplying unsanctioned findings.
END REFERENCE

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

04Reagents, instruments, and service coordinationThe service visit ended; H2 is still unavailable+

Your goal

Request a documented instrument-status decision and distinguish service attendance from authorized return to use.

This lesson in the book, page 31 ↗

A Read the situation

The service visit ended; H2 is still unavailable

Analyzer H2 is marked unavailable. A service visit ended at 11:00, but the laboratory lead has not confirmed return to service. Technician Noor asks lead Elena for the status needed before updating the clinic. No repair steps, instrument settings, reagent findings, patient results, or confirmed restart time are supplied. The end of the service visit is a known event, not evidence that the analyzer is available.

Noor: Laboratory technician / Elena: Laboratory lead

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is H2's current recorded status?

Answer and explanation

A. Unavailable

H2 is explicitly marked unavailable, with no confirmed return to service.

2. What happened at 11:00?

Answer and explanation

A. The service visit ended

Eleven o'clock is only the recorded end of the service visit.

3. What does Noor need before the clinic update?

Answer and explanation

A. The lead's confirmed status

Noor needs the laboratory lead's documented status rather than an inference from the visit.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

The service visit ended at 11:00, but H2 remains marked unavailable. A departure time is not return-to-service authorization, proof of a completed repair, or a guaranteed restart time.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
analyzer
Instrument used to perform laboratory measurements.identify the analyzer
instrument status
Recorded condition or availability of equipment.confirm instrument status
unavailable
Not available for the stated operational use.retain unavailable status
service visit
Attendance by personnel providing equipment support.record the service visit
return to service
Authorized restoration of equipment to operational use.confirm return to service
service report
Record describing work or findings from a service event.review the service report
maintenance log
Record of equipment upkeep and related actions.consult the maintenance log
equipment identifier
Reference distinguishing one instrument from another.quote the equipment identifier
downtime
Period when equipment is not available for its intended use.report downtime
restart authorization
Permission to resume operation under the relevant process.obtain restart authorization
reagent
Substance used in a laboratory reaction or analytical process.identify the reagent
reagent lot
Identified production batch of a reagent.record the reagent lot
lot number
Reference identifying a particular production batch.verify the lot number
expiry date
Date limiting use under applicable product instructions.check the expiry date
inventory status
Recorded availability of supplies.check inventory status
calibration
Establishing the relation between instrument response and reference values.document calibration
verification check
Check confirming a specified requirement is met.record a verification check
preventive maintenance
Planned upkeep intended to reduce equipment problems.schedule preventive maintenance
corrective maintenance
Work addressing an identified equipment problem.document corrective maintenance
service provider
Organization or person supplying equipment support.contact the service provider
operational readiness
State meeting the requirements for intended operation.confirm operational readiness
status confirmation
Explicit verification of the current equipment condition.request status confirmation
outstanding review
Assessment not yet completed or resolved.identify outstanding review
dilution factor
Ratio of final diluted volume to the original sample volume.verify whether the factor was applied

Request the lead's confirmed status and report only the authorized message. Do not add repair steps, settings, calibration results, reagent causes, or patient findings that are not supplied in the record.

C Notice the language

Name the status, not the hopeful implication

A completed visit can sound like a completed repair and approved restart. Keep those events separate. Ask the lead for the documented operating status rather than interpreting a service departure time as permission to use equipment.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Identify the instrument
I am checking the status of analyzer H2.
State the current record
H2 is marked unavailable.
Name the known event
The service visit ended at eleven.
Separate the implication
That does not confirm return to service.
Ask for authority
Has the lead confirmed operational availability?
Request the record
What status is documented for the clinic update?
Avoid a restart promise
I have no confirmed restart time.
Keep repair findings separate
The visit end does not tell me what repair was completed.
Avoid inventing a reagent issue
No reagent finding is supplied here.
Avoid technical improvisation
I am not changing settings based on this conversation.
Preserve the equipment reference
Please keep H2 in the status message.
Name the pending step
Return-to-service confirmation is still outstanding.
Limit the clinic message
I will report the current documented availability.
Separate service and testing
A service visit is not permission to resume analysis.
Request explicit notification
Please notify me when the authorized status changes.
Close precisely
Visit ended at eleven; H2 remains unavailable pending confirmation.
6 language and precision notes
Marked
Attributes status to the record rather than a personal impression.
Ended at
Gives an event time without implying operational approval.
Does not confirm
Separates evidence of attendance from evidence of readiness.
Has ... confirmed
Requests a completed authorized decision.
No ... supplied
Keeps technical findings outside the stated evidence.
When ... changes
Makes a later update conditional on an actual status change.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which clinic update is supported?

Answer and explanation

A. H2 remains unavailable; the service visit ended at 11:00 but return is unconfirmed

The supplied facts distinguish completed service attendance from unresolved operational status.

2. What would establish a status change here?

Answer and explanation

A. The laboratory lead's confirmed return-to-service status

The lead has not yet confirmed return, so that explicit status is needed.

3. Which detail must not be invented?

Answer and explanation

C. A restart time

No restart time is supplied by the known service event.

4. What does the service visit prove?

Answer and explanation

B. Support personnel attended and the visit ended

Attendance and its end are the only service facts established in the scenario.

Check explanations in the book, page 91 ↗

E Conversations

The service visit ended; H2 is still unavailable

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Noor: Laboratory technician / Elena: Laboratory lead

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • equipment identifier
  • service visit
  • service report
  • Operational readiness
  • Return to service
  • reagent lot
  • Calibration
  • status confirmation
  • downtime
  • instrument status
  1. Noor
    The clinic saw that service on H2 ended at eleven. They're asking if we can use the analyzer again. Its status still says unavailable.
  2. Elena
    Keep the 1 separate from the visit time. I have not confirmed return to service, so unavailable remains the status you can report.
  3. Noor
    Does the completed visit establish anything about availability, or only that the service attendance has finished?
  4. Elena
    Correct. A 2 can end without establishing operational readiness. We need the relevant documented outcome and authorized decision, not an inference from departure.
  5. Noor
    I'll remove eleven as the restart time. Can you confirm that return to use is still outstanding?
  6. Elena
    Exactly. 3 has not been confirmed. Please do not turn a known event time into a commitment we have not made.
  7. Noor
    Which document should the authorized reviewer check for the work completed? I don't have the service findings.
  8. Elena
    The 4 is relevant to the authorized review, but do not invent its contents. No repair steps or completed verification findings are supplied in this exchange.
  9. Noor
    The clinic is asking if this was a reagent problem. I don't see a reagent batch or fault finding in the supplied information.
  10. Elena
    Good. A 5 reference would be a specific technical claim. We have no such finding here, and it should not be added to make the explanation sound complete.
  11. Noor
    Someone has also described calibration as passed. We don't have that result from the completed visit, do we?
  12. Elena
    Correct. 6 status and settings belong to the applicable technical process. This conversation is about accurate coordination, not an instruction to perform or bypass that work.
  13. Noor
    Then my update is H2 unavailable, service visit ended at eleven, and no confirmed restart time. I won't give a duration for the remaining outage.
  14. Elena
    That describes the 7 without inventing its cause or duration. We can acknowledge the completed visit while preserving the unresolved operating status.
  15. Noor
    If the next message just says service done, I'll still need to ask whether the operational status changed.
  16. Elena
    Yes. Ask for 8 tied to H2. The wording should tell you whether availability changed, not merely that a person completed a task.
  17. Noor
    I'll retain H2 in the subject and message. An update about another instrument would not resolve this enquiry.
  18. Elena
    The 9 should remain visible. That is especially important when multiple service enquiries are being handled at once.
  19. Noor
    Please send the documented status change through the proper route when available. Until then, I'll keep the clinic's message at unavailable.
  20. Elena
    That is accurate. 10 remains unconfirmed. I will communicate a documented status change through the proper route when one is available.

Answers and explanations
  1. instrument status Turn 2. Instrument status remains unavailable until the lead confirms a documented change, regardless of the service visit ending.
  2. service visit Turn 4. Service visit records support attendance and its completion, not automatic approval that the instrument is operational.
  3. Return to service Turn 6. Return to service is the authorized restoration of operational use and has not been confirmed for H2.
  4. service report Turn 8. Service report can document service work or findings, but its contents and resulting authorization must be reviewed rather than assumed.
  5. reagent lot Turn 10. Reagent lot identifies a production batch and is not a documented cause of H2's unavailability in this scenario.
  6. Calibration Turn 12. Calibration is a technical measurement-related process whose successful completion is not established by the service visit ending.
  7. downtime Turn 14. Downtime is the period of unavailability, which remains ongoing in the supplied status despite the completed visit.
  8. status confirmation Turn 16. Status confirmation explicitly establishes the operating state needed for a clinic update rather than relying on a vague service-complete message.
  9. equipment identifier Turn 18. Equipment identifier H2 keeps the status attached to the correct analyzer and prevents confusion with another instrument.
  10. Operational readiness Turn 20. Operational readiness requires the relevant confirmed requirements for use and has not been established for H2.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Noor: The clinic saw that service on H2 ended at eleven. They're asking if we can use the analyzer again. Its status still says unavailable.
  2. Elena: Keep the instrument status separate from the visit time. I have not confirmed return to service, so unavailable remains the status you can report.
  3. Noor: Does the completed visit establish anything about availability, or only that the service attendance has finished?
  4. Elena: Correct. A service visit can end without establishing operational readiness. We need the relevant documented outcome and authorized decision, not an inference from departure.
  5. Noor: I'll remove eleven as the restart time. Can you confirm that return to use is still outstanding?
  6. Elena: Exactly. Return to service has not been confirmed. Please do not turn a known event time into a commitment we have not made.
  7. Noor: Which document should the authorized reviewer check for the work completed? I don't have the service findings.
  8. Elena: The service report is relevant to the authorized review, but do not invent its contents. No repair steps or completed verification findings are supplied in this exchange.
  9. Noor: The clinic is asking if this was a reagent problem. I don't see a reagent batch or fault finding in the supplied information.
  10. Elena: Good. A reagent lot reference would be a specific technical claim. We have no such finding here, and it should not be added to make the explanation sound complete.
  11. Noor: Someone has also described calibration as passed. We don't have that result from the completed visit, do we?
  12. Elena: Correct. Calibration status and settings belong to the applicable technical process. This conversation is about accurate coordination, not an instruction to perform or bypass that work.
  13. Noor: Then my update is H2 unavailable, service visit ended at eleven, and no confirmed restart time. I won't give a duration for the remaining outage.
  14. Elena: That describes the downtime without inventing its cause or duration. We can acknowledge the completed visit while preserving the unresolved operating status.
  15. Noor: If the next message just says service done, I'll still need to ask whether the operational status changed.
  16. Elena: Yes. Ask for status confirmation tied to H2. The wording should tell you whether availability changed, not merely that a person completed a task.
  17. Noor: I'll retain H2 in the subject and message. An update about another instrument would not resolve this enquiry.
  18. Elena: The equipment identifier should remain visible. That is especially important when multiple service enquiries are being handled at once.
  19. Noor: Please send the documented status change through the proper route when available. Until then, I'll keep the clinic's message at unavailable.
  20. Elena: That is accurate. Operational readiness remains unconfirmed. I will communicate a documented status change through the proper route when one is available.

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

The engineer left at eleven

Fictional service-status call using the H2 case.

  1. Technician: The maintenance record shows the visit ended at eleven. Is H2 confirmed back in service?
  2. Laboratory lead: Not yet. The completed visit doesn't establish the laboratory's readiness decision for testing.
  3. Technician: The clinic asked for a status. Should I say the instrument is still unavailable?
  4. Laboratory lead: Yes, that is the current status. Do not describe the service visit as permission to resume.
  5. Technician: They also asked whether an illuminated screen means the problem has been resolved.
  6. Laboratory lead: A visible screen doesn't establish our required readiness checks. Keep the update to confirmed status.
  7. Technician: Who will provide the next update so the clinic isn't relying on another visual guess?
  8. Laboratory lead: I will. I haven't confirmed a time, so please don't invent one for them.
  9. Technician: I'll say H2 remains unavailable, the service visit is complete, and your review is outstanding.
  10. Laboratory lead: That is accurate. Send equipment-operation questions to me rather than attempting to explain the repair.
Received does not mean verified

Fictional reagent shipment; lot V8 received but lot verification is incomplete.

  1. Technician: The new reagent lot V8 is here. Has its verification status been confirmed?
  2. Stock coordinator: Receipt is logged, but the required lot verification is still pending with the laboratory lead.
  3. Technician: Then I'll describe it as received, not available for testing on the strength of delivery alone.
  4. Stock coordinator: Please do. The purchase order's completed status refers to the delivery, not the technical review.
  5. Technician: Could you identify the lot in the update? There is another batch with similar packaging.
  6. Stock coordinator: Yes, I'll use V8 explicitly and avoid referring to it as just the new box.
  7. Technician: I also need to know whether a different lot is available; can you confirm that?
  8. Stock coordinator: I can't confirm another lot's status from this entry. The lead needs to check that separately.
  9. Technician: I'll ask the lead about availability without treating the two batches as interchangeable.
  10. Stock coordinator: That keeps the stock enquiry precise and avoids turning a receipt record into a use decision.
A service reference without patient details

Fictional support call about a display message; no patient data or repair steps are shared.

  1. Technician: I'm following up on service reference H19 for analyzer H2. Has your engineer accepted the enquiry?
  2. Service coordinator: Yes, an engineer is assigned, but we haven't confirmed a visit or resolution time.
  3. Technician: Our clinic team needs an update. Can I quote an assigned engineer rather than a scheduled visit?
  4. Service coordinator: Exactly. Assignment confirms ownership, not travel arrangements or completion of the repair.
  5. Technician: Do you need patient results in this call, or only the equipment reference and reported display message?
  6. Service coordinator: Only the approved equipment information for this enquiry. Don't send patient details with the service reference.
  7. Technician: I'll use the designated support channel and the description already recorded by our lead.
  8. Service coordinator: Please keep the message wording exact rather than paraphrase it as a confirmed technical diagnosis.
  9. Technician: Understood. Engineer assigned, visit unconfirmed; the cause remains with the service review.
  10. Service coordinator: I'll update the laboratory lead through the existing ticket when there is a confirmed scheduling change.

F Say it

  2. Switch roles. Read the lines rejecting an unsupported restart time, reagent fault and passed calibration; retain the missing findings.

Lesson 04: Reagents, instruments, and service coordination
Original fictional case: Analyzer H2 is marked unavailable. A service visit ended at 11:00, but the laboratory lead has not confirmed return to service. Technician Noor asks lead Elena for the status needed before updating the clinic. No repair steps, instrument settings, reagent findings, patient results, or confirmed restart time are supplied. The end of the service visit is a known event, not evidence that the analyzer is available.
Language workshop: The service visit ended; H2 is still unavailable
Communication goal: Request a documented instrument-status decision and distinguish service attendance from authorized return to use.
Language guidance: The service visit ended at 11:00, but H2 remains marked unavailable. A departure time is not return-to-service authorization, proof of a completed repair, or a guaranteed restart time.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): I am checking the status of analyzer H2. / H2 is marked unavailable. / The service visit ended at eleven. / That does not confirm return to service. / Has the lead confirmed operational availability? / What status is documented for the clinic update? / I have no confirmed restart time. / The visit end does not tell me what repair was completed. / No reagent finding is supplied here. / I am not changing settings based on this conversation. / Please keep H2 in the status message. / Return-to-service confirmation is still outstanding. / I will report the current documented availability. / A service visit is not permission to resume analysis. / Please notify me when the authorized status changes. / Visit ended at eleven; H2 remains unavailable pending confirmation.
Vocabulary: analyzer: Instrument used to perform laboratory measurements. / instrument status: Recorded condition or availability of equipment. / unavailable: Not available for the stated operational use. / service visit: Attendance by personnel providing equipment support. / return to service: Authorized restoration of equipment to operational use. / service report: Record describing work or findings from a service event. / maintenance log: Record of equipment upkeep and related actions. / equipment identifier: Reference distinguishing one instrument from another. / downtime: Period when equipment is not available for its intended use. / restart authorization: Permission to resume operation under the relevant process. / reagent: Substance used in a laboratory reaction or analytical process. / reagent lot: Identified production batch of a reagent. / lot number: Reference identifying a particular production batch. / expiry date: Date limiting use under applicable product instructions. / inventory status: Recorded availability of supplies. / calibration: Establishing the relation between instrument response and reference values. / verification check: Check confirming a specified requirement is met. / preventive maintenance: Planned upkeep intended to reduce equipment problems. / corrective maintenance: Work addressing an identified equipment problem. / service provider: Organization or person supplying equipment support. / operational readiness: State meeting the requirements for intended operation. / status confirmation: Explicit verification of the current equipment condition. / outstanding review: Assessment not yet completed or resolved. / dilution factor: Ratio of final diluted volume to the original sample volume.
Speaking task: Check the dialogue and read H2 unavailable beside service visit ended at eleven. Stress which statement describes the instrument. Switch roles. Read the lines rejecting an unsupported restart time, reagent fault and passed calibration; retain the missing findings. Complete the four-line exchange. End with return to service unconfirmed, not operational readiness established.
Partner: Noor: Laboratory technician / Elena: Laboratory lead

Noor: Please send the documented status change through the proper route when available. Until then, I'll keep the clinic's message at unavailable.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

05Turnaround times and realistic updatesThe two-hour clock starts at receipt+

Your goal

Clarify a turnaround-time starting point and distinguish a quoted interval from a confirmed reporting promise during a backlog.

This lesson in the book, page 40 ↗

A Read the situation

The two-hour clock starts at receipt

The service quotes a two-hour turnaround measured from laboratory receipt to reporting. Specimen T5 was collected at 08:00 and received by the laboratory at 09:00. A backlog affects estimates, and no report time is confirmed. Clinic coordinator Eva assumes the two hours began at collection and expects a report at 10:00. Technician Noor explains the receipt-based interval without replacing the mistaken 10:00 promise with an unsupported 11:00 guarantee.

Noor: Laboratory technician / Eva: Clinic coordinator

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. When does the quoted turnaround begin?

Answer and explanation

D. Laboratory receipt

The service explicitly measures the quoted interval from laboratory receipt to reporting.

2. When was T5 received?

Answer and explanation

D. 09:00

The specimen was collected at eight but received by the laboratory at nine.

3. What report time is confirmed?

Answer and explanation

A. None

The backlog affects estimates and the scenario supplies no confirmed report time.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Collection at 08:00 and receipt at 09:00 are different events. Applying the quoted two-hour interval to receipt gives 11:00 as the arithmetic reference, not 10:00. It still does not confirm a report time.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
turnaround time
Interval between defined starting and ending events; abbreviated TAT.clarify turnaround time
collection time
Recorded time the specimen was obtained.distinguish collection time
receipt time
Recorded time the laboratory received the specimen.confirm receipt time
reporting time
Time a report is issued under the relevant process.confirm reporting time
start point
Event from which an interval is measured.name the start point
end point
Event at which a measured interval ends.define the end point
quoted interval
Stated expected duration under the service description.explain the quoted interval
estimate
Approximate expectation subject to relevant uncertainty.qualify the estimate
confirmed deadline
Explicitly established time commitment.distinguish a confirmed deadline
backlog
Accumulated work awaiting completion.explain the backlog
work queue
Ordered or managed set of pending tasks.check the work queue
elapsed time
Time that has passed since a specified event.calculate elapsed time
transport interval
Time between the relevant dispatch and receipt events.distinguish the transport interval
receipt-to-report interval
Duration measured from laboratory receipt to reporting.explain the receipt-to-report interval
service standard
Defined service expectation under its stated conditions.clarify the service standard
estimated completion
Predicted finish time rather than a confirmed outcome.qualify estimated completion
delay notice
Communication explaining a later-than-expected process.issue a delay notice
update commitment
Promise to communicate status at a stated time.separate an update commitment
result commitment
Promise that a result will be available at a stated time.avoid an unsupported result commitment
queue status
Current position or condition of pending work.report queue status
time reference
Stated event and clock time used in a message.preserve the time reference
expectation reset
Correction of an earlier timing assumption.make an expectation reset
escalation route
Authorized path for raising a concern requiring further attention.use the escalation route
confirmed information
Details established by the relevant record or authority.relay confirmed information

The backlog affects estimates, so do not promise 11:00 merely because the starting point is now correct. Keep any status-update commitment separate from a result promise, and use the actual escalation route for clinical urgency concerns.

C Notice the language

Correct the clock and the certainty level

A timing misunderstanding may involve both the starting event and the strength of a promise. Explain each separately. A calculation from a quoted interval is not the same as a confirmed delivery time, especially when a stated backlog affects estimates.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Identify the specimen
This timing enquiry concerns T5.
State collection
Collection was recorded at eight.
State receipt
Laboratory receipt was recorded at nine.
Define the service interval
The quoted two hours run from receipt to reporting.
Correct the starting point
Collection does not start this service's quoted clock.
Explain the arithmetic
Nine plus two hours gives eleven as the reference calculation.
Limit the calculation
That is not a confirmed reporting promise.
Name the delay factor
A backlog is affecting estimates.
Preserve uncertainty
No report time is confirmed.
Avoid replacing one promise
I cannot change an unsupported ten-o'clock promise into an eleven-o'clock guarantee.
Separate an update
A status update is not a promise that results will be ready.
Ask for the right information
We need the current authorized estimate, if available.
Keep the clinical boundary
Urgency concerns need the applicable clinical escalation route.
Avoid reassurance without evidence
I cannot promise the delay is clinically unimportant.
Correct the clinic message
Please state receipt-based turnaround and the current uncertainty.
Close with the facts
Collected at eight, received at nine, reporting time unconfirmed.
6 language and precision notes
From ... to
Defines both ends of the measured interval.
Reference calculation
Separates arithmetic from an actual service commitment.
Affecting estimates
Explains why the quoted duration does not settle the current case.
If available
Avoids implying a current estimate already exists.
Not a promise
Limits the meaning of a status update.
Current uncertainty
Keeps the unresolved reporting time explicit after correcting the starting point.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Why is the assumed 10:00 time unsupported?

Answer and explanation

D. It starts the two-hour interval at collection rather than receipt

The clinic uses the wrong starting event for the stated receipt-to-report interval.

2. What does 09:00 plus two hours establish?

Answer and explanation

B. An 11:00 reference calculation, not a confirmed report time

Arithmetic applies the quoted interval correctly but does not remove the stated uncertainty.

3. Which update is accurate?

Answer and explanation

A. T5 arrived at nine; backlog affects estimates; no report time is confirmed

The correct update uses the receipt time and preserves the unresolved estimate.

4. What is distinct from a result commitment?

Answer and explanation

C. An agreed time to provide a status update

An update can communicate progress or continued delay without promising completed results.

E Conversations

The two-hour clock starts at receipt

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Noor: Laboratory technician / Eva: Clinic coordinator

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • turnaround time
  • escalation route
  • receipt time
  • update commitment
  • backlog
  • expectation reset
  • quoted interval
  • time reference
  • confirmed deadline
  • reference calculation
  1. Eva
    I told the clinic T5 would be reported at ten: collected at eight plus the quoted two hours. Have I used the right starting point?
  2. Noor
    The quoted 1 starts at laboratory receipt, not collection. T5 was received at nine, and a backlog is currently affecting estimates.
  3. Eva
    Then the nine-o'clock laboratory entry matters for that interval. I'll keep the eight-o'clock collection event in the record as a separate fact.
  4. Noor
    Correct. The 2 is the relevant starting point here. It is important to keep both events in the record rather than replacing one with the other.
  5. Eva
    Nine plus two gives eleven. Is eleven an actual reporting commitment, or only what the quoted interval would calculate?
  6. Noor
    No. That is a 3, not a confirmed reporting promise. No report time is confirmed, and the backlog means the estimate cannot be treated as guaranteed.
  7. Eva
    I need to withdraw the guarantee as well as correct the arithmetic. Simply replacing ten with eleven would keep the certainty error.
  8. Noor
    Exactly. The 4 explains how timing is described, but it does not by itself establish when this particular report will be issued under the current conditions.
  9. Eva
    I'll tell the clinic I used collection instead of receipt and gave a promise the available status didn't support.
  10. Noor
    That is a clear 5. It preserves the known collection and receipt facts while withdrawing the unsupported ten-o'clock commitment.
  11. Eva
    Can I attribute the uncertainty to a confirmed backlog, or would that be another assumption?
  12. Noor
    The 6 is a stated factor affecting estimates. We should not add an exact queue position, duration, or cause beyond the information actually available.
  13. Eva
    If we later agree an update time, I'll say update rather than report ready. No replacement update time has been agreed here.
  14. Noor
    Yes. An 7 is different from a result commitment. No new update time is supplied in this discussion, so we should not invent one either.
  15. Eva
    If the clinic raises clinical urgency, whom should that concern go through? I can't assess it from these timestamps.
  16. Noor
    Use the applicable 8. We must not dismiss urgency or give clinical reassurance merely because the quoted interval was misunderstood.
  17. Eva
    My correction will retain collection at eight, receipt at nine, and the quoted two-hour receipt-to-report interval, with no report time confirmed.
  18. Noor
    That keeps the 9 explicit. Anyone reading the note can see which event starts the quoted interval and which timing remains unknown.
  19. Eva
    I'll withdraw ten o'clock and avoid promising eleven. Any actual reporting commitment needs its own confirmation.
  20. Noor
    Agreed. A 10 is not supplied here. Keep the calculation, estimate, and any later authorized commitment distinct in subsequent messages.

Answers and explanations
  1. turnaround time Turn 2. Turnaround time for this service is measured from laboratory receipt to reporting, not from specimen collection.
  2. receipt time Turn 4. Receipt time is 09:00 for T5 and is the starting event specified in this service's quoted interval.
  3. reference calculation Turn 6. Reference calculation applies the quoted interval to receipt but does not establish a confirmed report time during the backlog.
  4. quoted interval Turn 8. Quoted interval is the service's stated receipt-to-report duration, which is distinct from a guaranteed time for this specimen.
  5. expectation reset Turn 10. Expectation reset corrects both the collection-based assumption and the unsupported reporting guarantee in the earlier message.
  6. backlog Turn 12. Backlog is explicitly supplied as affecting estimates, while the exact report time remains unconfirmed.
  7. update commitment Turn 14. Update commitment promises communication of status, which may still be pending, rather than guaranteeing that a report is ready.
  8. escalation route Turn 16. Escalation route directs clinical urgency concerns to the authorized process rather than inferring their significance from turnaround arithmetic.
  9. time reference Turn 18. Time reference links each clock time to its actual event so collection and receipt are not confused again.
  10. confirmed deadline Turn 20. Confirmed deadline would require an explicit established reporting commitment, which this scenario does not provide.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Eva: I told the clinic T5 would be reported at ten: collected at eight plus the quoted two hours. Have I used the right starting point?
  2. Noor: The quoted turnaround time starts at laboratory receipt, not collection. T5 was received at nine, and a backlog is currently affecting estimates.
  3. Eva: Then the nine-o'clock laboratory entry matters for that interval. I'll keep the eight-o'clock collection event in the record as a separate fact.
  4. Noor: Correct. The receipt time is the relevant starting point here. It is important to keep both events in the record rather than replacing one with the other.
  5. Eva: Nine plus two gives eleven. Is eleven an actual reporting commitment, or only what the quoted interval would calculate?
  6. Noor: No. That is a reference calculation, not a confirmed reporting promise. No report time is confirmed, and the backlog means the estimate cannot be treated as guaranteed.
  7. Eva: I need to withdraw the guarantee as well as correct the arithmetic. Simply replacing ten with eleven would keep the certainty error.
  8. Noor: Exactly. The quoted interval explains how timing is described, but it does not by itself establish when this particular report will be issued under the current conditions.
  9. Eva: I'll tell the clinic I used collection instead of receipt and gave a promise the available status didn't support.
  10. Noor: That is a clear expectation reset. It preserves the known collection and receipt facts while withdrawing the unsupported ten-o'clock commitment.
  11. Eva: Can I attribute the uncertainty to a confirmed backlog, or would that be another assumption?
  12. Noor: The backlog is a stated factor affecting estimates. We should not add an exact queue position, duration, or cause beyond the information actually available.
  13. Eva: If we later agree an update time, I'll say update rather than report ready. No replacement update time has been agreed here.
  14. Noor: Yes. An update commitment is different from a result commitment. No new update time is supplied in this discussion, so we should not invent one either.
  15. Eva: If the clinic raises clinical urgency, whom should that concern go through? I can't assess it from these timestamps.
  16. Noor: Use the applicable escalation route. We must not dismiss urgency or give clinical reassurance merely because the quoted interval was misunderstood.
  17. Eva: My correction will retain collection at eight, receipt at nine, and the quoted two-hour receipt-to-report interval, with no report time confirmed.
  18. Noor: That keeps the time reference explicit. Anyone reading the note can see which event starts the quoted interval and which timing remains unknown.
  19. Eva: I'll withdraw ten o'clock and avoid promising eleven. Any actual reporting commitment needs its own confirmation.
  20. Noor: Agreed. A confirmed deadline is not supplied here. Keep the calculation, estimate, and any later authorized commitment distinct in subsequent messages.

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Which clock starts at nine?

Fictional T5 timing call using the lesson case.

  1. Clinic coordinator: T5 was collected at eight. Doesn't the two-hour turnaround time mean reporting by ten?
  2. Technician: This service measures turnaround from laboratory receipt to reporting. Receipt was at nine, not eight.
  3. Clinic coordinator: Then should I promise the clinician a report at eleven instead of ten?
  4. Technician: No. The backlog affects the estimate, so changing the starting point doesn't guarantee eleven.
  5. Clinic coordinator: Could the section lead provide a current estimate rather than just repeat the usual interval?
  6. Technician: Yes, I'll ask for the latest timing information with T5's reference and receipt time.
  7. Clinic coordinator: We need to distinguish an overdue prediction from a specimen that hasn't arrived.
  8. Technician: Receipt is confirmed. The open question is reporting time, not whether the laboratory has T5.
  9. Clinic coordinator: I'll explain that to the clinician and avoid describing collection time as your receipt time.
  10. Technician: I'll pass on the timing query and keep any new estimate clearly labeled.
The add-on is a new request

Fictional clinic requests extra testing on an existing specimen; no technical suitability decision.

  1. Clinic nurse: The specimen is already with you. Can we add the extra test without another discussion?
  2. Technician: An add-on request still needs the appropriate order and specimen-suitability review.
  3. Clinic nurse: I thought an existing accession meant every additional test could use the same material.
  4. Technician: It identifies the case, but doesn't establish that the specimen meets every new test's requirements.
  5. Clinic nurse: Could you send the add-on request to the section lead for that review?
  6. Technician: Yes. I'll keep it linked to the original accession while distinguishing the additional requested test.
  7. Clinic nurse: Please tell us whether it was accepted rather than assuming the original testing approval covers it.
  8. Technician: That's the status I'll request. Acceptance and reporting time will need separate confirmation.
  9. Clinic nurse: We haven't told the patient to return for another collection; that decision isn't made.
  10. Technician: I'll preserve that uncertainty and report the lead's decision, without adding collection instructions myself.
A priority label needs confirmation

Fictional clinician enquiry says urgent; recorded order priority has not been checked.

  1. Technician: Your message says STAT priority, but I need to clarify the recorded order priority.
  2. Clinic coordinator: The clinician asked for an urgent update. I may have used STAT to mean that.
  3. Technician: An urgent request for information isn't automatically a changed testing priority. Let's distinguish them.
  4. Clinic coordinator: We need the current status first, and the clinician can clarify any priority request.
  5. Technician: I'll route that question to the laboratory lead with the order reference.
  6. Clinic coordinator: Please don't change the clinical request based only on my shorthand in this telephone message.
  7. Technician: I won't. I'll describe your message as an urgent status enquiry, with order priority to verify.
  8. Clinic coordinator: Does that wording mean you are deciding it isn't clinically urgent?
  9. Technician: No. I'm not making that assessment; I'm identifying the information that needs the responsible team's clarification.
  10. Clinic coordinator: Thank you. I'll ask the clinician to confirm the intended priority through the proper order route.

Answer and explanation

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Scope: All cases and local procedures are fictional. This is English practice, not medical advice or laboratory operating instruction. Lesson 05: Turnaround times and realistic updates
Original fictional case: The service quotes a two-hour turnaround measured from laboratory receipt to reporting. Specimen T5 was collected at 08:00 and received by the laboratory at 09:00. A backlog affects estimates, and no report time is confirmed. Clinic coordinator Eva assumes the two hours began at collection and expects a report at 10:00. Technician Noor explains the receipt-based interval without replacing the mistaken 10:00 promise with an unsupported 11:00 guarantee.
Language workshop: The two-hour clock starts at receipt
Communication goal: Clarify a turnaround-time starting point and distinguish a quoted interval from a confirmed reporting promise during a backlog.
Language guidance: Collection at 08:00 and receipt at 09:00 are different events. Applying the quoted two-hour interval to receipt gives 11:00 as the arithmetic reference, not 10:00. It still does not confirm a report time.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): This timing enquiry concerns T5. / Collection was recorded at eight. / Laboratory receipt was recorded at nine. / The quoted two hours run from receipt to reporting. / Collection does not start this service's quoted clock. / Nine plus two hours gives eleven as the reference calculation. / That is not a confirmed reporting promise. / A backlog is affecting estimates. / No report time is confirmed. / I cannot change an unsupported ten-o'clock promise into an eleven-o'clock guarantee. / A status update is not a promise that results will be ready. / We need the current authorized estimate, if available. / Urgency concerns need the applicable clinical escalation route. / I cannot promise the delay is clinically unimportant. / Please state receipt-based turnaround and the current uncertainty. / Collected at eight, received at nine, reporting time unconfirmed.
Vocabulary: turnaround time: Interval between defined starting and ending events; abbreviated TAT. / collection time: Recorded time the specimen was obtained. / receipt time: Recorded time the laboratory received the specimen. / reporting time: Time a report is issued under the relevant process. / start point: Event from which an interval is measured. / end point: Event at which a measured interval ends. / quoted interval: Stated expected duration under the service description. / estimate: Approximate expectation subject to relevant uncertainty. / confirmed deadline: Explicitly established time commitment. / backlog: Accumulated work awaiting completion. / work queue: Ordered or managed set of pending tasks. / elapsed time: Time that has passed since a specified event. / transport interval: Time between the relevant dispatch and receipt events. / receipt-to-report interval: Duration measured from laboratory receipt to reporting. / service standard: Defined service expectation under its stated conditions. / estimated completion: Predicted finish time rather than a confirmed outcome. / delay notice: Communication explaining a later-than-expected process. / update commitment: Promise to communicate status at a stated time. / result commitment: Promise that a result will be available at a stated time. / queue status: Current position or condition of pending work. / time reference: Stated event and clock time used in a message. / expectation reset: Correction of an earlier timing assumption. / escalation route: Authorized path for raising a concern requiring further attention. / confirmed information: Details established by the relevant record or authority.
Speaking task: Check the answers. Read collection at eight and receipt at nine as separate events, then calculate nine plus two as eleven. Switch roles. Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. REFERENCE
Course: Medical Laboratory Technician English
Professional audience: Medical laboratory technicians and clinical laboratory support staff
Scope: All cases and local procedures are fictional. This is English practice, not medical advice or laboratory operating instruction. Follow applicable privacy rules, validated procedures, professional scope, and authorized escalation. Lesson 05: Turnaround times and realistic updates
Original fictional case: The service quotes a two-hour turnaround measured from laboratory receipt to reporting. Specimen T5 was collected at 08:00 and received by the laboratory at 09:00. A backlog affects estimates, and no report time is confirmed. Clinic coordinator Eva assumes the two hours began at collection and expects a report at 10:00. Technician Noor explains the receipt-based interval without replacing the mistaken 10:00 promise with an unsupported 11:00 guarantee.
Language workshop: The two-hour clock starts at receipt
Communication goal: Clarify a turnaround-time starting point and distinguish a quoted interval from a confirmed reporting promise during a backlog.
Language guidance: Collection at 08:00 and receipt at 09:00 are different events. Applying the quoted two-hour interval to receipt gives 11:00 as the arithmetic reference, not 10:00. It still does not confirm a report time.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): This timing enquiry concerns T5. / Collection was recorded at eight. / Laboratory receipt was recorded at nine. / The quoted two hours run from receipt to reporting. / Collection does not start this service's quoted clock. / Nine plus two hours gives eleven as the reference calculation. / That is not a confirmed reporting promise. / A backlog is affecting estimates. / No report time is confirmed. / I cannot change an unsupported ten-o'clock promise into an eleven-o'clock guarantee. / A status update is not a promise that results will be ready. / We need the current authorized estimate, if available. / Urgency concerns need the applicable clinical escalation route. / I cannot promise the delay is clinically unimportant. / Please state receipt-based turnaround and the current uncertainty. / Collected at eight, received at nine, reporting time unconfirmed.
Vocabulary: turnaround time: Interval between defined starting and ending events; abbreviated TAT. / collection time: Recorded time the specimen was obtained. / receipt time: Recorded time the laboratory received the specimen. / reporting time: Time a report is issued under the relevant process. / start point: Event from which an interval is measured. / end point: Event at which a measured interval ends. / quoted interval: Stated expected duration under the service description. / estimate: Approximate expectation subject to relevant uncertainty. / confirmed deadline: Explicitly established time commitment. / backlog: Accumulated work awaiting completion. / work queue: Ordered or managed set of pending tasks. / elapsed time: Time that has passed since a specified event. / transport interval: Time between the relevant dispatch and receipt events. / receipt-to-report interval: Duration measured from laboratory receipt to reporting. / service standard: Defined service expectation under its stated conditions. / estimated completion: Predicted finish time rather than a confirmed outcome. / delay notice: Communication explaining a later-than-expected process. / update commitment: Promise to communicate status at a stated time. / result commitment: Promise that a result will be available at a stated time. / queue status: Current position or condition of pending work. / time reference: Stated event and clock time used in a message. / expectation reset: Correction of an earlier timing assumption. / escalation route: Authorized path for raising a concern requiring further attention. / confirmed information: Details established by the relevant record or authority.
Speaking task: Check the answers. Read collection at eight and receipt at nine as separate events, then calculate nine plus two as eleven. Switch roles. Read the correction withdrawing the ten-o'clock promise; do not replace it with an eleven-o'clock guarantee. Complete the four-line exchange. Keep a status update separate from a report-ready commitment, and retain the unconfirmed reporting time.
Partner: Noor: Laboratory technician / Eva: Clinic coordinator
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Complete the T5 timing summary. Clock values identify known events or arithmetic only.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Eva: My correction will retain collection at eight, receipt at nine, and the quoted two-hour receipt-to-report interval, with no report time confirmed.
Noor: That keeps the time reference explicit. Anyone reading the note can see which event starts the quoted interval and which timing remains unknown.
Eva: I'll withdraw ten o'clock and avoid promising eleven. Any actual reporting commitment needs its own confirmation.
Noor: Agreed. A confirmed deadline is not supplied here. Keep the calculation, estimate, and any later authorized commitment distinct in subsequent messages.
END REFERENCE
06Preliminary, final, and amended reportsCorrecting the ten-o'clock report label+

Your goal

Withdraw an inaccurate report-status statement and identify the superseding final version without inventing changed findings or an amendment.

This lesson in the book, page 49 ↗

A Read the situation

Correcting the ten-o'clock report label

The clinic received a preliminary report at 10:00 and a final report at 14:00 for the same request. Technician Noor mistakenly called the earlier version final. Clinic contact Priya asks which report status is correct. The 14:00 final report supersedes the 10:00 preliminary version for this request. No findings, clinical conclusions, or subsequent amended report are supplied. Noor needs to correct the earlier communication clearly and retain a traceable record.

Noor: Laboratory technician / Priya: Clinic contact

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What was received at 10:00?

Answer and explanation

A. A preliminary report

The earlier report is explicitly preliminary, despite Noor's mistaken description.

2. Which version supersedes it?

Answer and explanation

A. The 14:00 final report

The supplied final report at fourteen hundred supersedes the preliminary version for this request.

3. What has not been supplied?

Answer and explanation

C. Findings or a later amended report

The scenario provides report stages and times but no findings or later amendment.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

The 10:00 report was preliminary; calling it final was a communication error. The 14:00 final report supersedes that version. This sequence does not establish what any findings say or whether their content changed.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
preliminary report
Report issued before the relevant final reporting stage.identify the preliminary report
final report
Report issued with final status under the applicable process.confirm the final report
amended report
Formally revised report issued through the applicable correction process.distinguish an amended report
supersede
Replace an earlier version as the applicable later record.supersede the preliminary version
report version
Identified edition or stage of a report.check the report version
issue time
Recorded time a report was issued.quote the issue time
status label
Wording identifying a report's current stage.correct the status label
communication correction
Explicit repair of an inaccurate spoken or written message.make a communication correction
detection limit
Lowest amount reliably detectable under the stated method conditions.distinguish detection from quantification
version control
Management of identifiable document revisions and their relationships.preserve version control
current report
Report currently applicable under the stated reporting process.identify the current report
superseded version
Earlier record replaced by an applicable later version.retain the superseded version appropriately
correction trail
Record linking an error to its documented correction.preserve the correction trail
recipient acknowledgment
Confirmation that the intended recipient received the message.obtain recipient acknowledgment
report discrepancy
Difference in report information or status requiring clarification.clarify the report discrepancy
finding
Recorded result or observation in a report.avoid inventing a finding
interpretation
Explanation of the meaning of a finding.refer interpretation appropriately
addendum
Additional report information issued under the applicable process.distinguish an addendum
revision reason
Documented explanation for a report change.state the revision reason
reissue
Issuance of a report again under the applicable procedure.verify the reissue status
distribution record
Record of where a report or update was sent.check the distribution record
document history
Record of earlier versions and changes.preserve document history
explicit correction
Statement directly identifying and fixing the prior error.provide an explicit correction
quantification limit
Lowest amount quantified with suitable precision and accuracy.retain the below-quantification qualifier

An amended report is a formally revised report under the relevant process. Correcting Noor's inaccurate label does not prove such a report exists. Preserve the report history and follow the actual record-correction arrangements rather than deleting the earlier version.

C Notice the language

Name the correction directly

A vague later update may leave the original mistaken statement in circulation. Identify the earlier label, withdraw it explicitly, and state the correct version and time. Correcting communication is not the same as claiming the laboratory issued an amended clinical report.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Open the correction
I need to correct my earlier description.
Name the error
I called the ten-o'clock report final.
Withdraw the label
That label was incorrect.
State the correct status
The ten-o'clock report was preliminary.
Identify the later version
The fourteen-hundred report is final.
Explain the relationship
The final report supersedes the preliminary version for this request.
Avoid a content claim
I am not saying the findings changed.
Avoid inventing an amendment
No later amended report is supplied here.
Separate processes
This is a correction to my communication.
Request acknowledgment
Please confirm you have the corrected status.
Preserve time references
Keep ten and fourteen attached to their respective versions.
Maintain history
Retain the earlier version according to the record policy.
Identify the current reference
Please use the final version's status in this enquiry.
Avoid clinical interpretation
No clinical conclusion is part of this correction.
Make follow-up traceable
Link the correction to the earlier message.
Close precisely
Ten was preliminary; fourteen is final and supersedes it.
6 language and precision notes
I called
Takes responsibility for the inaccurate description.
Was incorrect
Withdraws the earlier label without ambiguity.
Supersedes
Explains the relationship between versions, not the meaning of findings.
Not saying
Prevents a status correction from becoming a content claim.
No later ... supplied
Limits the known report sequence.
Respective
Keeps each time paired with its own version.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which correction is clear?

Answer and explanation

B. I incorrectly called the 10:00 report final; it was preliminary; the 14:00 report is final

The correct message identifies the error and gives both accurate version statuses.

2. What does supersedes mean here?

Answer and explanation

D. The later final version replaces the preliminary version for this request

Superseding describes which version is applicable, not deletion or a clinical conclusion.

3. Which claim is unsupported?

Answer and explanation

B. An amended report was issued after 14:00

No later amendment appears in the supplied report sequence.

4. What should be preserved?

Answer and explanation

C. A traceable correction and report history under policy

A documented correction should repair the message without concealing the relevant history.

E Conversations

Correcting the ten-o'clock report label

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Noor: Laboratory technician / Priya: Clinic contact

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • amended report
  • finding
  • explicit correction
  • document history
  • current report
  • issue time
  • communication correction
  • correction trail
  • status label
  • receipt
  1. Noor
    Priya, I need to correct my earlier message. I called the ten-o'clock report final. It was preliminary; the fourteen-hundred report is final.
  2. Priya
    Thank you for the 1. I'll correct my note before passing the earlier label on.
  3. Noor
    Please keep both issue times. The ten-o'clock document wasn't final at issue, even though a final version now exists.
  4. Priya
    I'll change the earlier 2 to preliminary and identify fourteen hundred as the final version. I won't relabel the original document itself.
  5. Noor
    Right. I'm correcting what I said about its status, not announcing a new laboratory finding.
  6. Priya
    So this is a 3. It repairs your message but does not, by itself, tell me that any clinical content changed.
  7. Noor
    Exactly. Nor should we call the fourteen-hundred final report amended merely because I used the wrong word earlier.
  8. Priya
    An 4 would need its own formal status and record. The supplied sequence is preliminary followed by final.
  9. Noor
    Can you repeat the two versions back? I want to make sure the earlier mistake is not carried forward.
  10. Priya
    Ten hundred: preliminary. Fourteen hundred: final. I'll retain each 5 beside the corresponding status.
  11. Noor
    That's correct. We haven't been given the clinical content of either version, so I can't describe a change in the patient's result.
  12. Priya
    I won't infer a changed 6 from the status change. The supplied evidence concerns the report versions and your earlier wording.
  13. Noor
    Please link this correction to the inaccurate message rather than silently replacing it. The next reader should know what was corrected.
  14. Priya
    I'll preserve the 7 through the applicable record process. That keeps the original error and its correction traceable.
  15. Noor
    The earlier report also belongs in the retained version record under policy. Superseded does not mean erase the history.
  16. Priya
    Understood. The 8 stays intact while the fourteen-hundred final version is identified as current for this request.
  17. Noor
    Can you confirm that you've received the corrected version information, rather than only seen that I sent a follow-up?
  18. Priya
    I confirm 9: ten hundred preliminary, fourteen hundred final, and your earlier final label for ten hundred is withdrawn.
  19. Noor
    Thank you. No later amendment is established here. I'll use the same version wording in any onward communication.
  20. Priya
    I'll refer to the fourteen-hundred final version as the 10, without inventing findings or hiding the earlier correction.

Answers and explanations
  1. explicit correction Turn 2. Explicit correction directly identifies the earlier inaccurate final label instead of leaving the recipient to infer what changed.
  2. status label Turn 4. Status label for the earlier report must be preliminary, correcting Noor's mistaken description without inventing clinical content.
  3. communication correction Turn 6. Communication correction repairs Noor's inaccurate message and does not itself establish an amended clinical report.
  4. amended report Turn 8. Amended report would be a formally revised report under the applicable process, which the stated preliminary-to-final sequence does not establish.
  5. issue time Turn 10. Issue time distinguishes the 10:00 preliminary report from the 14:00 final report in the correction and handoff.
  6. finding Turn 12. Finding refers to report content, which has not been supplied and must not be inferred from a change in report status.
  7. correction trail Turn 14. Correction trail links the inaccurate message to its explicit replacement so the earlier error remains traceable.
  8. document history Turn 16. Document history retains the version sequence and correction record according to policy instead of hiding the earlier report or error.
  9. receipt Turn 18. Receipt establishes that Priya received the corrected version information rather than leaving communication assumed.
  10. current report Turn 20. Current report is the supplied 14:00 final version applicable to this request, with no later amendment established.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Noor: Priya, I need to correct my earlier message. I called the ten-o'clock report final. It was preliminary; the fourteen-hundred report is final.
  2. Priya: Thank you for the explicit correction. I'll correct my note before passing the earlier label on.
  3. Noor: Please keep both issue times. The ten-o'clock document wasn't final at issue, even though a final version now exists.
  4. Priya: I'll change the earlier status label to preliminary and identify fourteen hundred as the final version. I won't relabel the original document itself.
  5. Noor: Right. I'm correcting what I said about its status, not announcing a new laboratory finding.
  6. Priya: So this is a communication correction. It repairs your message but does not, by itself, tell me that any clinical content changed.
  7. Noor: Exactly. Nor should we call the fourteen-hundred final report amended merely because I used the wrong word earlier.
  8. Priya: An amended report would need its own formal status and record. The supplied sequence is preliminary followed by final.
  9. Noor: Can you repeat the two versions back? I want to make sure the earlier mistake is not carried forward.
  10. Priya: Ten hundred: preliminary. Fourteen hundred: final. I'll retain each issue time beside the corresponding status.
  11. Noor: That's correct. We haven't been given the clinical content of either version, so I can't describe a change in the patient's result.
  12. Priya: I won't infer a changed finding from the status change. The supplied evidence concerns the report versions and your earlier wording.
  13. Noor: Please link this correction to the inaccurate message rather than silently replacing it. The next reader should know what was corrected.
  14. Priya: I'll preserve the correction trail through the applicable record process. That keeps the original error and its correction traceable.
  15. Noor: The earlier report also belongs in the retained version record under policy. Superseded does not mean erase the history.
  16. Priya: Understood. The document history stays intact while the fourteen-hundred final version is identified as current for this request.
  17. Noor: Can you confirm that you've received the corrected version information, rather than only seen that I sent a follow-up?
  18. Priya: I confirm receipt: ten hundred preliminary, fourteen hundred final, and your earlier final label for ten hundred is withdrawn.
  19. Noor: Thank you. No later amendment is established here. I'll use the same version wording in any onward communication.
  20. Priya: I'll refer to the fourteen-hundred final version as the current report, without inventing findings or hiding the earlier correction.
